# GTX 1070 + i7-4770K: Qwen3.6-35B-A3B MoE + MTP decode and prefill optimization

Status: accepted on 2026-10-29 by the agent's own autonomous acceptance under the owner's grant (NOT owner approval; scope below).  Re-checked and re-stated on 2026-10-30 against a new closing profile of the final build (H67, D42). Commits (local, not pushed): `a18ddca` engine changes, `ce12a91` docs and tools, and the commit that adds this status line.

## Autonomous mode

The owner set this goal with the chat command `/goal continue working on the experimental optimization autonomously until
there is no hypotheses to explore` on 2026-10-29. Under `AGENTS.md` ("Autonomous Mode") that makes autonomous mode apply to this goal
until it is accepted or the owner says stop. The agent decides, records and accepts by the contract below, and labels
the acceptance as its own, never as owner approval. Never autonomous, and therefore not done here: pushes, pull requests, system or
security settings (CPU governor, transparent hugepage mode, `perf_event_paranoid`, driver counters), permanent deletion.
The owner's earlier request put PR #6 on GitHub (before this grant); everything after it stays in local commits.

## Goal and observable outcome

Make the owner's baseline server command faster on this machine for decode (tok/s with MTP) and for prefill (tok/s, time to first
token), without changing what the model computes. The baseline is the command of `tools/pascal/moe_bench.py` (`BASE`): Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-UD-Q4_K_M,
`--hybrid-moe --lm-prefill ... --spec-type draft-mtp ... --spec-mtp-defer`, `-c 262000`, q8_0 KV, one GTX 1070 (8 GiB), i7-4770K (4 cores, 8
threads), 30 GB RAM. Starting point (build `final-bin` / `rem-bin`, remainder off): decode 32.6 / 31.3 / 35.3 tok/s on three chats and 24.8 after a
16k prompt; prefill 471 tok/s at 4k and 574 at 16k.

## Acceptance contract (written before the runs of this goal)

Claims the work may make: speed ratios of one build or setting against a control, on this machine, this model, the harness prompts
(`build/ctx100k/base.txt` cut by tokens; three fixed chats; 256 decode tokens), measured A-B-B-A. No claim about quality beyond bit-identity or
the recorded text-hash comparison; no perplexity claim; no claim for other hardware, models or the Windows build.

Criteria for KEEPING a change (all must hold, decided before seeing results; a change to a criterion is recorded as a decision before the rerun):

- K1 speed: the targeted metric's B mean beats the A mean by at least 2% in an A-B-B-A session, and the worst B arm beats the best A arm
  (the two A arms and the two B arms do not overlap). For decode the metric is ms per verify round (eval time / (tokens - accepted drafts)) when the generated
  text differs between arms, tok/s when it is identical.
- K2 no collateral loss: no other metric (decode tok/s, prefill tok/s at 4k and 16k, peak VRAM) is worse by more than 1% (VRAM: +50 MiB) unless the loss is
  recorded in the decision log as an explicit trade-off with its reason.
- K3 exactness: either bit-identical (the op-level checker finds 0 differences, or the text hashes agree in all arms) or the change is labelled
  TEXT-CHANGING (a different set of resident experts changes the rounding of some experts) and K1 uses ms per round.
- K4 gates (below) pass, and the server logs of the arms show no error, abort, OOM or memory-guard decline that the control does not show.
- K5 documented: `docs/guide/LINUX-PASCAL.md`, `docs/guide/PARAMETERS.md` (if a flag or variable), `docs/currentstate.md` and the ledger below.

Default rule: a change is on by default only if it is exact (K3 without the label), K1 holds in at least two independent sessions or at two prompt lengths,
and it cannot affect another GPU architecture (gated by an architecture check). Otherwise it is an opt-in flag or variable, or only advice.

Gates (commands run from the worktree root; expected result):

- G1 `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0 build/llama-cuda/bin/test-backend-ops test -o MUL_MAT_ID -b CUDA0` and `-o MUL_MAT`: all pass (887 / 1,218 at the start; more with new cases). Once per remainder mode when `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_REMAINDER` is touched.
- G2 `build/llama-cuda/bin/test-moe-lean-expert`: 0 failures (3,295 checks at the start), after any change to `llama-moe-lean.h`.
- G3 `build/pascal/check_mmq_remainder`: 0 comparisons differ (30 at the start), after any change to the MMQ launch.
- G4 `tools/pascal/abba.sh TAG A_BIN B_BIN --pp 4096,16384 --deep 16384 --chats 3 --tg 256 --hlog` with `ARGS_A` / `ARGS_B` for per-arm options; the table of `abba_table.py` is the evidence.
- G5 the GPU is free before every arm (`moe_bench.py` refuses to start otherwise); GPU 0 only; no build, test or host analysis while an arm runs.

Stop condition (the goal is accepted when): every hypothesis of the ledger is resolved (KEPT, REJECTED with evidence, or BLOCKED by a never-autonomous action
with what is lost), AND a closing profile of decode and of prefill (nsys, as in `tools/pascal/README.md`) shows no kernel category of 5% or more of its phase
without a resolved hypothesis. If the closing profile exposes one, it becomes a ledger entry and the work continues.

Validation of the closing profile: `nsys profile` of one decode arm and one prefill arm through `moe_bench.py --wrap`, tables by `tools/dense/nsys-prefill.py` and the decode script in this file's history (currentstate 2026-10-24).

## Method

Screening: single arms of `moe_bench.py` (`--pp "" --deep 16384 --chats 3 --tg 256 --hlog`, side-copied binaries via `--bin`) queued one after the other, with a control arm first and
last in the queue (drift check), compared by `tools/pascal/arms_table.py`. A screening result under 2% is noise (the control spread is 0.1 to 1%). A candidate that passes screening goes to a G4 session.

Sampled `scaling_cur_freq` and the utilisation of all 8 logical CPUs every 0.2 s during a 4-chat decode with the stack (233 samples, 1,864 per-CPU points, `build/pascal/h15.csv`, git-ignored): CPUs with utilisation >= 0.8 in the interval ran at a median 3,898 MHz in 801 points, 100% at 3,700 MHz or more (minimum 3,755 MHz); at >= 0.5 it is 99.0% (1,087 points), at >= 0.3 98.1% (1,164 points); the few low readings are the first samples after a core wakes, while the governor ramps. The busiest CPU of each sample was at 3,700 MHz or more in 97.4% of the samples. A `performance` governor can only remove that ramp, so on this machine the headroom is bounded by the share of busy points below the maximum (1-2%). Limit: this is a frequency record, not an A/B of the governor; an A/B would need the system change, which this work does not make (a machine where the governor does not reach the maximum is another matter) |

| H27 | Gated delta net prefill kernel with fewer lanes per state column (`gated_delta_net_quad`: 4 lanes per column, 32 rows per lane in registers, 2 shuffles per reduction, k and q of 8 tokens staged in shared memory; opt-in `GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD=1`, 16 tokens or more, scalar gate, no snapshots) | prefill +3 to +5% (9.8% of kernel time, op 2x faster) | code, medium | KEPT (opt-in env) | op level (`perf -o GATED_DELTA_NET`, 16 heads, 2048 tokens): generic 10,149 us; quad 4 lanes / 8 tokens 4,744 us (2.14x); sweep lanes 2 / 4 / 8 x tokens per stage: 7.1-7.7 / 4.7-5.0 / 6.4-6.7 ms (4 lanes x 16 tokens 4,651 us, 2% better than 8); `GATED_DELTA_NET` eval 39/39 vs the CPU reference. Order of the sums differs from the generic kernel: TEXT-CHANGING label. A-B-B-A `H27` (h10-bin, the stack, `GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD=1` in B): 4k prompt 508.5 -> 533.5 tok/s (+4.9%), the 16k prompt 604.5 -> 642.9 (+6.3%), no A/B overlap; peak VRAM 7,299 -> 7,325 / 7,345 MiB. The 128-token decode after the 16k prompt gave a different text: 32.34 -> 31.32 tok/s (-3.2%), acceptance 0.909 -> 0.886, hit rate 0.629 -> 0.585, ms/round +6.4% (one sample; the kernel is not used in decode, so this is a text effect). H27b (A-B-B-A, four chats of 128 tokens whose 30 to 65-token prompts also run the kernel, and the decode after a 12k prompt): prefill of the 12k prompt 606.7 -> 645.8 tok/s (+6.4%); decode tok/s -1.8 / +0.6 / -0.8 / +4.3 / +0.5%, ms/round -0.4 / -0.6 / +3.1 / -4.1 / -2.0% (mean -0.8%; with the +6.4% of the first session the six ms/round samples average +0.4%): no systematic decode effect, text differs in every cell; peak VRAM 7,319 -> 7,383 MiB (+64 MiB, cause not isolated). Numerical check, `llama-perplexity --kl-divergence` (4 chunks of 2,048 tokens, ub 512, `-ncmoe 37`, generic kernel as the base): quad 4 lanes: mean KLD 0.01145 +- 0.00046, same top token 94.0%, RMS dp 2.3%, PPL 18.72 vs 18.78 (ln ratio -0.003 +- 0.003). Controls: generic rerun and `-ub 256` bit-identical (KLD 0, 100% same top); quad with 16 tokens per stage (same order of sums) KLD 0.0000 with 99.98% of the top tokens equal (2 of ~8,200 differ); quad with 2 and with 8 lanes against quad 4 lanes: KLD 0.01145 / 0.01163. A change of the order of the sums in every delta net layer gives the same ~0.0115 whichever order it is (the engine notes: activation re-quantization in every matmul amplifies a rounding-level difference of every element to one floor), while a sparse difference gives ~0; so the quad kernel is as far from the generic one as any other order is from it, and PPL is unchanged within noise. Status: opt-in (not exact, so not a default under the default rule) |

Inside the 2.0% drift of the control and under the 2% bar |

A fused multiply-and-sum would remove the multiply's pass: 433 ms = 1.6% of the pass, under the 2% bar. | H28 | Flash attention (tile kernel) is the largest prefill category in the closing profile: 21.5% of kernel time at 16k tokens (`flash_attn_tile<256,256,32,1>` 5,125 ms, n=200, mean 25.6 ms). Removing only the PV multiply-adds cuts the int8-QK kernel by 37% (74.0 -> 46.4 ms at 10,240 keys, 259 -> 163 ms at 34,816), so PV is the lever: an int8 PV with `dp4a` (P quantized per column, V requantized per tile to one scale per 32 head-dim values and transposed to words of 4 keys) | attention -25% (-4% prefill at 16k, -11% at 49k) | code, large | KEPT (env `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV`: opt-in until H49, default 1 under `--hybrid-moe` since D27; text-changing) | Built in `fattn-tile.cuh` (`flash_attn_tile_pv_i8`; needs `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8QK=1`, q8_0 K and V, head size 256, config 0; the V cache is read raw, so the f16 V conversion pass and its scratch disappear). Op level (`FLASH_ATTN_EXT` hs 256, GQA 8, 2,048 queries, q8_0 KV, `test-backend-ops perf`): 10,240 keys 74.2 -> 59.8 ms (-19.4%), 34,816 keys 262.5 -> 215.5 ms (-17.9%). Bugs found on the way: the lane-to-head-dim mapping of this build copies 8 bytes at a time (a lane holds pairs of d in four chunks, not two chunks of four): a standalone comparison on fixed data (`tools/pascal/fa_pv8_check.cpp`) found it (NMSE 2.0 -> 3e-5). Quality, three P variants (first-token KL of the top 20 against exact FP32 attention, the layer-major pass of a plain-decode server, `tools/pascal/ftk_arm.py`; repeats are bit-identical): (1) round-to-nearest P is NOT safe: 4k / 12k mean KL 0.0010 but one of two 32k prompts 0.147 (top-1 logprob off by 0.244 nats; mean 0.0745), because every weight below 0.4% of the tile maximum is dropped, a bias; (2) two-byte P (hi + lo) fixes it (0.0035 on that prompt) but is no faster than FP32 PV (75.8 against 74.2 ms, 184 B spills at 128 registers): rejected; (3) one byte with dithered (hash-driven, deterministic, unbiased) rounding: 4k / 12k mean KL 0.0040 (4 prompts, max top-1 shift 0.041), 32k 0.0039 / 0.0064 (max shift 0.093), 55k 0.0040 (shift 0.040); int8 QK alone is 0.0034 / 0.0026 / 0.0191 (shifts up to 0.116). Same top-1 token in all 7 prompts. The kept variant is (3). End to end, A-B-B-A `PV8` (one binary, env off against on, the stack, prefill only): 4k 519.7 -> 522.9 tok/s (+0.6%), 16k 621.8 -> 642.5 (+3.3%), 32k 536.0 -> 570.2 (+6.4%), no A/B overlap at 16k and 32k; peak VRAM 7,343-7,345 -> 7,299 MiB in this session but 7,299 -> 7,317 / 7,379 MiB in the repeat (poller noise around the cache resize, no systematic change). Repeat session `PV8b` (final build): 16k 621.0 -> 639.0 tok/s (+2.9%), 32k 534.1 -> 562.8 (+5.4%), 49k 452.5 -> 489.6 (+8.2%), no overlap at any length. A 55k-token prompt on the plain server, single runs: exact attention 165.1 s, int8 QK 125.2 s, int8 QK + int8 PV 114.3 s (-8.7%). Not bit-identical, so opt-in (default rule). Limits: 7 first-token points, no perplexity; prompts beyond 55k and the hybrid-server text were not compared More first-token points on the final build (8k and 24k tokens at three text offsets, six prompts): mean KL against exact attention 0.0186 for int8 QK alone and 0.0176 with the int8 PV (individual prompts up to 0.0435 and 0.090; one top-1 flip for int8 QK, none with the PV); pooled over all 13 points the mean KL is 0.0115 (int8 QK) and 0.0105 (int8 QK + dithered PV), so the PV adds no systematic error. QK-phase ablations (int8 QK + int8 PV, 10,240 keys, base 61.2 ms; 34,816 keys base 221.7 ms): without the Q shared-memory loads 50.5 ms / 181.9 (they cost 17.6% / 18%), without the K loads 55.5 / 200.3 (9.3% / 9.7%); replacing the dp4a by integer xor-adds is slower (63.9 / 228.9), so the arithmetic is not the cost: the QK phase is bound by the shared-memory reads of Q (a broadcast of 16 bytes per column, block and half, for every key) and K. Ways to cut the Q reads: more columns per warp (64 per block, built, 122-159 ms: slower, registers) or two keys per lane (needs 64 keys per tile: cfg 3 and 4 spill 488 / 1,960 B and are 3.5x slower; an int8-PV version would also need the V tile processed in two halves); the upper bound of halving the Q reads is 8.8% of the kernel (about 1.5% of a 16k prefill, 2% at 32k), below the 2% bar for the work, so it is not built QK reuse, built after the ablations: with the int8 PV the QK phase is 16.9 of 60.8 ms (10,240 keys) and 55.8 of 213.7 ms (34,816); a proxy that doubled the K reads and dp4a per Q read of the existing kernel added only 6.3 / 28.4 ms, so two keys per lane were expected to save 8.7% / 6.4% of the kernel. Built as a 64-key tile (configuration 3) with the int8 PV in two 32-key halves, after restructuring the QK loop to load Q once per (column, block, half) for all a lane's keys (configuration 3 spilled 488 B and was 3.5x slower; now 24 B, 56 B with the PV; the 32-key kernels are bit-identical, NMSE 0 on fixed data): 10,240 keys 61.1 -> 55.3 ms (-9.5%), 34,816 keys 212.5 -> 193.5 (-9.0%); against the FP32 PV 74.2 -> 55.3 (-25.5%) and 262.5 -> 193.5 (-26.3%). It is the default of `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV=1` (`LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8CFG=0` gives the 32-key tile). End to end, A-B-B-A `PV8c` (final3-bin, off against on): 16k 618.3 -> 644.3 tok/s (+4.2%), 32k 530.6 -> 577.4 (+8.8%), 49k 451.7 -> 509.0 (+12.7%), no A/B overlap, peak VRAM 7,299 -> 7,301 MiB. Quality of the 64-key tile: 6 prompts of 8k / 24k tokens mean first-token KL 0.0200 (int8 QK alone 0.0186; the 32-key PV 0.0176), 2 prompts of 32k 0.0079 (0.0026; 0.0052), top-1 flip on one prompt (also flipped by int8 QK alone). `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` 2937/2937 in the default, int8-QK and int8-QK + PV modes. q4_0 K and V cache (`-ctk q4_0 -ctv q4_0`): the int8 PV is supported (V loaded as q4_0, abs(q) <= 8). The first version reused the q8_0 requantization scale, which squeezes the q4_0 values to a few levels: NMSE 1.2e-3 against the FP32 PV and 5 of 6 eval cases failing (ERR 0.001-0.004); mapping the tile's largest row to +-127 instead (scale x 8/127) gives NMSE 5e-5 and 6/6. Attention 73.3 -> 59.3 ms at 10,240 keys (-19%), 255 -> 216 ms at 34,816 (-15%); the 64-key tile is slower than the 32-key one with q4_0 (69.8 against 61.8 ms), so q4_0 defaults to the 32-key tile (`LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8CFG`). Perplexity-grade check (paired next-token log-likelihood of the layer-major pass, plain-decode server, final build, `tools/pascal/ftk_arm.py --ppl`; 54 of 58 positions have the true next token in the top 200 of every arm): 44 windows of 4,096 tokens, exact attention NLL 1.4008 (4.06 at these positions), int8 QK dNLL -0.021 +- 0.021, int8 QK + int8 PV (64-key tile) -0.022 +- 0.021; 10 windows of 16,384 tokens, exact 1.2440, int8 QK -0.043 +- 0.036, int8 QK + PV -0.109 +- 0.068; the true-token top-1 agreed with exact attention at 52/54 (int8 QK) and 51/54 (with the PV) positions: no detectable loss, at a resolution of about 0.02 nats (short windows) and 0.07 (long windows). Deep contexts, A-B-B-A `PV8d` (final build, a 750 KB text of repo documents, the stack, env off against on): 98,304 tokens 307.3 -> 368.0 tok/s (+19.8%; 320 s -> 267 s), 131,072 tokens 254.1 -> 310.6 (+22.2%; 515 s -> 422 s), no A/B overlap, peak VRAM 7,327 -> 7,335 MiB. The gain grows with depth because attention takes a larger share of the pass: +4.2% at 16k, +8.8% at 32k, +12.7% at 49k, +19.8% at 98k, +22.2% at 131k (the 16k / 32k / 49k session repeated: +4.2 / +8.8 / +12.8%, `PV8e`; 98k and 131k are one session). Quality at 98,304 tokens of context (one window of the long text, plain-decode server, final build; `chain21`): first-token KL against exact attention 0.00007 for int8 QK and 0.00024 with the int8 PV, same top token, dNLL of the true next token +0.012 and +0.023 on that single position; the prefill took 449 s with exact attention, 314 s with int8 QK and 262 s with the PV (-16.5% against int8 QK). Remaining items, measured: quality at 131,072 tokens (plain-decode server, prefill 743 s exact, 509 s int8 QK, 416 s int8 QK + PV): first-token KL 0.0194 (int8 QK) and 0.0191 (+ PV), NLL of the true next token 0.2926 exact, -0.025 and -0.014, same top token. A wider paired log-likelihood, 144 positions in 4,096-token windows (150 windows at step 500; 6 without the true token in the top 200 skipped): exact NLL 1.4841 (perplexity 4.41 at these positions), int8 QK +0.0053 +- 0.0086, int8 QK + PV +0.0037 +- 0.0079 (about +0.4% perplexity, resolution +-0.8%), top token equal to exact attention at 137/144 and 138/144 positions. q4_0 K and V cache on the hybrid server, A-B-B-A `PV8q4` (the 32-key tile): 16k 621.2 -> 638.7 tok/s (+2.8%), 49k 455.7 -> 491.3 (+7.8%), no A/B overlap; the same at 131,072 tokens, A-B-B-A `PV8q4b` (hybrid server, q4_0 K and V, 32-key tile): 268.2 / 260.4 (A) against 295.5 / 294.8 (B) tok/s, mean 264.3 -> 295.2 (+11.7%), no A/B overlap, peak VRAM 7,305-7,307 -> 7,305-7,363 MiB (poller noise around the cache resize), hit rate and cache size equal in all four arms. A second paired log-likelihood set on a different stretch of the text (`y`, `build/pascal/long.txt`, 150 windows of 4,096 tokens at step 1,300, final build, plain-decode server; 146 have the true token in the top 200 of every arm): exact NLL 1.8475, int8 QK -0.0246 +- 0.0447, int8 QK + PV -0.1266 +- 0.0764, top token equal to exact attention at 133/146 and 136/146 positions. This set is noisy: 18 of its positions have an exact NLL above 5 (digits inside number tables, e.g. NLL 9.3 for a '2'), three of them move by 5.6-8.0 nats in either direction (towards a better NLL in all three cases that exceed 5 nats), the median change is +0.0006 / -0.0001 and the trimmed mean (5 positions off each end) +0.0135 / -0.0056; the large moves are near-ties among digits, not a drift. Pooled with the first set (`z`, 144 windows), 290 positions: exact NLL 1.6670 (perplexity 5.30), int8 QK -0.0098 +- 0.0229, int8 QK + PV -0.0619 +- 0.0388, medians 0.0000 / -0.0001, top token equal at 270/290 (93.1%) and 274/290 (94.5%). Reading: no loss is detectable at a resolution of about +-0.05 nats (int8 QK) and +-0.08 (with the PV), i.e. +-5% to +-8% of perplexity for the pooled set (+-0.8% for the first set alone, which has fewer surprise tokens); a loss of 1% or less cannot be excluded. The measured price is the first-token distribution shift (KL 0.0001-0.04 over the prompts above) and the 5.5% to 6.9% of positions where the top token differs from exact attention (these are mostly near-ties; the true-token NLL is not worse on average, within the resolution above) |

The results are far from any plausible bar (+4.7%, clean, 0.1 point), so the late wording changes nothing. Commands: `tools/pascal/abba.sh` and `tools/pascal/moe_bench.py` as in H32, one arm at a time on GPU 0 after the sibling's H33 sessions (GPU.lock). Result. (1) A-B-B-A `H34a`, 65,536 tokens of depth, 256 decode tokens, the stack (p-min 0.7, 6 threads, `-fitt 512`) against p-min 0.9 + the MTP layer's experts on the GPU: tok/s 25.50 / 26.86 / 26.45 / 25.40, mean 25.45 -> 26.66 (+4.7%), ms per round 77.38 -> 62.78 (-18.9%), acceptance 0.846 -> 0.928, no overlap (A arms differ by 0.4%, B arms by 1.5%), cache 40 -> 34 slots at the start (51 -> 45 at the end) and hit rate 0.562 -> 0.518, peak VRAM 7,319 / 7,339 / 7,321 / 7,319 MiB, text differs. H33 measured p-min 0.9 alone at 64k as -0.4% on the same text; the first reading, that the MTP-experts flag carries the gain at 64k (it is +4.7% alone at 32k, H32c), was an inference by subtraction and H36 FALSIFIED it: the flag alone at 64k is 0.0%, so the +4.7% is an interaction of the two (see H36). (2) Gate `H34g`: one server with p-min 0.9, 6 threads, `-fitt 512` and the MTP experts on the GPU took prompts of 32,768 and 61,440 tokens (527.8 and 402.7 tok/s) and then a decode of 128 tokens at 32,791 tokens of depth (29.24 tok/s, 47 of 50 drafts accepted): exit code 0, peak VRAM 7,321 MiB, the 526-line server log has no error, abort, fail, illegal, out-of-memory, 'no room' or decline line, the cache resized between 21 and 45 slots (94-529 ms per resize). (3) `H34c`, int8 PV prefill, 98,304 tokens, the variable off against on, A-B-B-A: 307.20 / 367.70 / 367.50 / 307.00 tok/s, mean 307.1 -> 367.6 (+19.7%), the arms of one kind differ by 0.07% and 0.05%, peak VRAM 7,343 / 7,335 / 7,335 / 7,323 MiB, hit rate 0.2859 against 0.2852: replicates the earlier single session (+19.8%, 307.3 -> 368.0). The 131k session (+22.2%) is still one session. Claims allowed: these ratios for this machine, one deep text (the H32 / H33 prompt cut at 65,536 tokens) and 256 decode tokens; the gate is one server run, not a soak. |
| H35 | Is the 0.9-against-0.7 gain at depth a property of the text? H33 showed +3.2% / +6.7% at 32k / 49k and 0% at 24k / 64k, each a different prompt (the same text cut at different lengths) | text variation of the H32 advice | flag, harness only | REJECTED (both criteria failed; the p-min half of the H32 advice does not hold) | Contract. Outcome: the tok/s and ms-per-round ratio of p-min 0.9 against 0.7 at one depth, 32,768 tokens, for four different texts: `build/ctx100k/base.txt` (the H32a text, +3.2% known), `build/pascal/long.txt` (repo documents), a code text (`cat third_party/llama.cpp/src/llama-*.cpp`, cut at 32,768 tokens by the harness) and a second documents text (the guide files concatenated). Criteria, fixed now: the H32 advice (p-min 0.9 at depth) is KEPT if the mean tok/s ratio over the four texts is at least +1.5% AND no text loses more than 2% without overlap; otherwise the advice is REJECTED and the guide says that 0.9 is neutral at depth and 0.7 stays the value. ms per round is reported beside it for every text. Claims allowed: ratios for these four texts at 32k on this machine, nothing about other depths. Commands: `tools/pascal/abba.sh` with `--pp "" --chats 0 --deep 32768 --tg 256 --hlog --text FILE`, one text per session, after the sibling's H34 chain (GPU.lock). Result (A-B-B-A, the stack, `final4-bin`, 256 decode tokens, greedy, the text differs between the p-min arms): code text (`llama-*.cpp`) 30.93 -> 30.52 tok/s (-1.3%, no overlap: A 30.84-31.02, B 30.32-30.73), ms/round 73.90 -> 59.48 (-19.5%), acceptance 0.832 -> 0.935; library docs 30.12 -> 30.85 (+2.4%, no overlap), ms/round 67.99 -> 58.03 (-14.6%), acceptance 0.814 -> 0.919; guide prose 30.37 -> 29.31 (-3.5%, no overlap: A 30.34-30.39, B 29.25-29.38), ms/round 67.45 -> 55.27 (-18.0%), acceptance 0.923 -> 0.961; with the base text of H32a (+3.2%, ms/round -14.2%) the four texts give +3.2 / -1.3 / +2.4 / -3.5%, mean +0.2%. Both criteria failed (mean below +1.5%, and one text loses more than 2% without overlap), so the advice 'p-min 0.9 at depth' is rejected: p-min 0.9 shortens a verify round by 14-20% on every text and every depth from 24k and raises the acceptance of what is drafted, but it drafts fewer tokens per round, and whether tok/s goes up or down depends on the text (-3.5% to +3.2% at 32k; with the other depths -0.6% to +6.7%). 0.7 stays the value. Seven points (four texts at 32k, the base text at 24k / 49k / 64k) lean the sign of the gain towards the texts whose acceptance at 0.7 is already high (0.923 loses most; Pearson r -0.62, p about 0.14), a post-hoc reading with too few points to be a finding and not a hypothesis with a contract. Limits: one prompt per text, 256 tokens per arm, four texts, this machine. |

H34 inferred that the flag carries the +4.7% of the deep recipe at 64k (p-min 0.9 alone was -0.4% there) and H35 showed that the other half of the recipe is text-dependent; the flag alone (p-min 0.7 kept) has been measured at 32k on one text (+4.7%, H32c) and as +5.6% over eight shallow chats | +2% at 64k and no text loss | flag, harness only | KEPT by its criteria (mean +3.3%), with a 64k exception: the flag alone is 0.0% there | Contract. The flag helps on two more texts at 32k (+4.3 / +5.6%, the texts on which p-min 0.9 alone lost 3.5% / 1.3%), so at 32k it is text-robust where 0.9 is not. At 64k it does nothing alone, while the combination with p-min 0.9 gave +4.7% in H34a (flag alone 25.30, 0.9 alone 25.41 per H33, both together 26.66 tok/s, ms per round 62.8 against 76.7 and ~69.6): an interaction between two sessions' worth of single-factor runs, seen in three separate sessions on one text with a stable control (stack 25.29-25.50). Whether the combination also adds on the other texts is not measured (H38). Claims allowed: the three cells above. |
| H37 | Empty blocks in the MoE MMQ grid: the main `mul_mat_q` launch for the K-quant expert matmuls is (nty, ntx = ncols_max/64 = 32, 256 experts) = 8 x 32 x 256 = 65,536 blocks (Q4_K) and 32 x 32 x 256 = 262,144 (Q5_K) per call at a 2,048-token ubatch, while about 64 tokens per expert exist (16,384 token-expert pairs / 256), so at most ~512 tiles per row tile hold work and 96% or more of the blocks exit at once (guide section 5, stream-k idea, bounded from the profile `close-pp`: 5,600 ms of the 26,400 ms kernel time of a 16k prefill are these main launches, mean 3.5-4.6 ms for Q4_K) | up to a few % of the main launches = up to 0.6-2% of prefill | code, small to medium | KEPT (stage 1 passed, stage 2 built; default on for GPUs older than Volta, `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_ID_FLAT=0` turns it off; bit-identical) | Contract. Stage 1 (bound, instrument only): an env knob `LLAMA_MMQ_ID_NTX_CAP=N` in `launch_mul_mat_q` caps ntx for launches that have expert bounds (timing only: results are wrong for experts with more than N x 64 tokens, so it is used in `test-backend-ops perf -o MUL_MAT_ID` and never in a model run); the saving is the op time without against with the cap, both repeated three times. Stage 1 PASSES (go to stage 2) if the capped op is at least 3% faster than the uncapped op for both Q4_K and Q5_K shapes; below that the row is REJECTED by bound (the main launches are 21% of prefill kernel time, so 3% of them is 0.6% of prefill, under the 1% bar of this work). Stage 2 (only after a pass): a flat tile list so that the grid is nty x (an exact upper bound of non-empty tiles) instead of nty x ntx x n_experts, with bit-identical output (the per-tile arithmetic is unchanged): `test-backend-ops` MUL_MAT_ID 887/887 and the five existing quantization cases unchanged, `check_mmq_remainder` 30/30, and a prefill A-B-B-A at 4k and 16k with at least +1% and no overlap. Claims allowed: the op-level percentages of stage 1 on the test routing (uniform random), the prefill ratio of stage 2 on this machine. Commands: `test-backend-ops perf -o MUL_MAT_ID -b CUDA0 -p 'n_mats=256,n_used=8'` with and without the env, then `tools/pascal/abba.sh` for stage 2. One session at a time on GPU 0 after the sibling's H36 (GPU.lock). Result. Stage 1 (`test-backend-ops perf -o MUL_MAT_ID`, uniform routing, 3 repeats, mean us/run, the cap passed as LLAMA_MMQ_ID_NTX_CAP, timing only): Q4_K (m 512, n 2048, k 2048) 5,928 -> 5,748 with cap 2 (-3.0%) and 5,724 with cap 1 (-3.4%); Q5_K (m 2048, n 2048, k 512) 8,750 -> 6,070 (-30.6%) and 5,967 (-31.8%): PASSED (both shapes at 3% or more; Q4_K by a small margin), so about 3.3% of the prefill was in the empty blocks once weighted by the kernels' share (Q5_K main launches 9.8% of kernel time, Q4_K 11.3%). Stage 2: the main launch (tile mode 1 of the remainder split) takes a flat grid of the tiles that hold columns, `nty x n_flat` with n_flat = ncols_dst / 64 + n_experts at most (512 against 8,192 tile slots for 2,048 tokens), found by a one-block prefix-sum kernel (`mmq_tile_offsets`) over the experts' tile counts and a binary search in the kernel; used when it is smaller than the old grid and n_flat <= 65,535; the tile arithmetic is untouched. Op level, median of 3, flat off -> on, us/run, tokens 2048 / 1024 / 512 / 256 / 128: Q4_K 6,061 -> 5,781 (-4.6%), 3,291 -> 3,177 (-3.4%), 2,113 -> 2,059 (-2.5%), 1,572 -> 1,577 (+0.3%), 1,470 -> 1,456 (-0.9%); Q5_K 8,965 -> 6,274 (-30.0%), 4,936 -> 3,634 (-26.4%), 3,158 -> 2,555 (-19.1%), 2,273 -> 1,987 (-12.6%), 2,025 -> 1,901 (-6.1%). Gates: `test-backend-ops` MUL_MAT_ID 887/887 with the flat list on and off, MUL_MAT 1,218/1,218; bit-identical: the first-token top-20 log-probabilities of 6 prompts (4k / 8k / 16k tokens at two text offsets, plain-decode server) are exactly equal with the flat list off and on, and `check_mmq_remainder` (uniform and skewed routing) gives 30 comparisons with 0 differences and the same 10 case hashes with the flat list off and on. End to end, A-B-B-A `H37p` (one binary, `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_ID_FLAT=0` against the default, the stack): prefill 4,096 tokens 516.4 -> 535.0 tok/s (+3.6%), 16,384 tokens 617.4 -> 643.0 (+4.1%), no A/B overlap, peak VRAM 7,299-7,317 against 7,307 MiB. Reading: the old grid launched 8 x 32 x 256 = 65,536 blocks (Q4_K) and 32 x 32 x 256 = 262,144 (Q5_K) per call of which about 4% hold a full tile; each empty block waits for the expert-bounds read while it holds an SM slot, which costs most for the 32-row-tile Q5_K down projection. This is the cheaper cousin of the stream-k idea (it removes the empty-block cost, not the wave tail, which at 5,000 or more active tiles per call over 30-45 resident blocks is below 1%). Limits: uniform routing in the op test, prefill measured at 4k and 16k only (the gain is per 2,048-token chunk, deeper prompts were not run), one machine. |
| H39 | A larger prefill chunk after the flat tile list: `--lm-prefill-ubatch 4096` gave +1.2% at 4k and +0.4% at 16k before H37 (the empty blocks of the expert grid doubled with the chunk, 524,288 blocks for the Q5_K down matmul); with the grid now proportional to the work the chunk may pay more | +1-3% prefill | flag | REJECTED (the 16k criterion failed) | Contract. A-B-B-A `H39`, one binary (`h37-bin`, the flat tile list on by default), prefill 4,096 and 16,384 tokens, the stack: A the default chunk (`--lm-prefill-ubatch 2048`), B `--drop "--lm-prefill-ubatch 2048" --add "--lm-prefill-ubatch 4096"` (segment 4,096 and `-b 4096` as in the baseline). KEPT as advice if B is at least +2% at 16k tokens with no A/B overlap AND the peak VRAM of B is at most 300 MiB above A's AND no memory-guard decline or error appears in the B server logs; REJECTED otherwise. The cache size after the prefill (the table's 'cache N -> M slots') is reported beside it because decode after the prefill depends on it. Claims allowed: prefill tok/s ratios at 4k and 16k on this machine for one text. Command: `tools/pascal/abba.sh H39 build/pascal/h37-bin build/pascal/h37-bin --pp 4096,16384 --chats 0 --deep 0 --hlog` with ARGS_A / ARGS_B as above, after the sibling's H38 (GPU.lock). Result: prefill 4,096 tokens 538.7 -> 544.8 tok/s (+1.1%, no overlap: A 536.8-540.6, B 543.7-545.9), 16,384 tokens 649.3 -> 650.2 (+0.1%, overlap); peak VRAM 7,299-7,317 MiB in all four arms, cache 40 -> 51 slots in all four, no decline or error. The 16k bar (+2%) failed by 1.9 points: with the empty blocks gone the chunk size does not matter any more (the earlier +1.2% / +0.4% was within the same band). `--lm-prefill-ubatch 2048` stays. Limits: one text, 4k and 16k. |
| H40 | Decode at depth: a round costs 14 ms more at 49-64k tokens than at 8k (H33 sessions: 63.7 ms at 8k, 61.0 at 24k, 66.2 at 32k, 75.2 at 49k, 77.2 at 64k, p-min 0.7, same stack), 18-20% of the round, while the packed attention kernel did nothing (H32b) and the KV is read at about 1-2 ms per token at 256 GB/s: where does it go? | up to several % of decode at depth if a GPU kernel is the cause | instrument first | RESOLVED (the category is named: attention of the 3-4 row verify rounds, H44) | Contract. Instrument result only: nsys profiles (`--trace=cuda`) of the decode (192 tokens, the stack, `h37-bin`) after a deep prompt of 8,192 and of 49,152 tokens (`tools/pascal/moe_bench.py --wrap`, `build/pascal/slowprefill-h40.sh`). The decode window is everything after the last prefill kernel (`flash_attn_tile`); per decode token the GPU kernel time by category (flash attention, MMQ / MMVQ matvecs, copies and conversions, everything else) and the wall time per token are compared between the two depths. Decision rule, fixed now: if one GPU kernel category grows by 5 ms per round or more from 8k to 49k and is not at the card's bandwidth floor for its bytes, a kernel row with its own contract follows (this row is then RESOLVED by naming the category); if the growth of the GPU time is under 5 ms per round and the wall time grows by more, the depth penalty is host-side (CPU expert leg, synchronisation, cache hit rate) and the row is REJECTED as not fixable on the GPU side, with the numbers. Claims allowed: the per-token time split at these two depths on this machine for one prompt. Result (profiles `build/pascal/h40-d8192`, `h40-d49152`, git-ignored; 192 decode tokens, nsys on, window = after the last prefill MMQ kernel): decode window 6,404 -> 8,177 ms (33.4 -> 42.6 ms per token, +9.2), GPU kernel time 4,929 -> 6,582 ms (+8.6 ms per token = 93% of the growth; the GPU was busy 77% -> 81% of the window), so the depth penalty is on the GPU, not host-side. By kernel, total over the 192 tokens, 8k -> 49k: `dequantize_block` (q8_0 K/V to f16) 96.6 -> 612.3 ms (+516), `flash_attn_tile` 93.1 -> 471.8 (+379), `moe_hybrid_wait_add_f32` 1,498 -> 1,810 (+313, the spin wait for the host), `fattn_gqa8` 50.4 -> 239.7 (+189), the q8_0 / Q6_K matvecs +83 / +63 (about 9% more calls: lower acceptance, 93 of 120 drafts against 102), everything else under +20 each. The attention path (dequantize + tile + packed) is +1,084 ms = 66% of the GPU growth. The cause is the kernel selection: with `--spec-draft-n-max 3` a verify batch has 2 to 4 rows; for 1-2 rows the quantized-KV rule picks the vector path and the packed GQA kernel (`fattn_gqa8`, on by default with `--hybrid-moe`, 0.38 ms per layer-call at 49k), but for 3-4 rows (about 40% of the rounds: 790 tile calls = 39.5 rounds x 2 KV heads x 10 layers) it picks the tile kernel, which with `--fattn-head-tile` first converts the K and V of each KV head to f16 (0.39 ms per call, 4 calls per layer-round) and runs for 0.60 ms per head: 5.3 ms per 3-4-row round at 8k, 27.4 ms at 49k. That is the 14 ms per round of H33 (22 ms x 40% of the rounds = 8.7 ms on average, the rest the host wait and the extra rounds). The earlier 'GQA8 does nothing at 49k' (H32b) was right for what it measured: the rule never reached the 3-4-row rounds. Limit: one prompt per depth, profiler on, 192 tokens. |
| H44 | Route the 3-4 row verify batches to the packed GQA kernel (H40's cause): in the no-tensor-core branch of `ggml_cuda_get_best_fattn_kernel`, quantized K and V with 3 or 4 query rows return the vector path when `ggml_cuda_fattn_gqa8_applies` (the dispatch then runs `fattn_gqa8`, which serves 1-4 rows) instead of the tile kernel with its f16 conversion; `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=2` restores the old routing | up to ~9 ms of a 75 ms round at 49k (about +10% decode), about 0 at shallow depth | code, 4 lines | KEPT, default since H48 (`LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS`, default 4; it was opt-in until H47 / H48 showed that its KL criterion was below the floor of the test) | Contract. (a) Numerics: `test-backend-ops test -o FLASH_ATTN_EXT` passes unchanged (all cases, with `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1` and without), and the targeted q8_0 head-256 GQA-8 cases with 3 and 4 query rows are among those run and pass. (b) Speed: decode A-B-B-A, one binary, the stack, 256 tokens, A `--env LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=2` (old routing) against B the default, at 49,152 tokens of depth and at 8,192: KEPT if at 49k ms per round is at least 8% lower and tok/s at least +3% with no A/B overlap, AND at 8k tok/s is not more than 1% below A's (text may differ, so ms per round is judged beside tok/s) AND the draft acceptance of B is within 0.03 of A's in both cells (a bound on a numerics change); REJECTED otherwise, with the numbers. (c) A third cell, 64k tokens, is run only if the 49k cell passes. Claims allowed: the ratios at these depths for one deep prompt on this machine. Commands: `tools/pascal/abba.sh` with `--pp "" --chats 0 --deep D --tg 256 --hlog` after a server build; GPU after the sibling's H42/H43 chain (GPU.lock). First results (build `h44-bin`, A `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=2`, B the default): (a) numerics: `test-backend-ops` FLASH_ATTN_EXT 2,940/2,940 with `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1` and 2,940/2,940 without (the case list now includes 4 rows: nb 2, 3, 4 at 512 / 2,048 / 8,192 keys). (b) 49,152 tokens: tok/s 26.19 -> 29.07 (+11.0%, no overlap: A 26.03-26.34, B 28.88-29.26), ms/round 75.79 -> 69.34 (-8.5%), acceptance 0.814 -> 0.801 (-0.013): the primary criteria pass. 8,192 tokens: tok/s 31.83 -> 33.77 (+6.1%, no overlap), ms/round 64.86 -> 61.14 (-5.7%), acceptance 0.868 -> 0.805 (-0.063): the per-cell acceptance bound of 0.03 FAILED at 8k, as written. Decision D21, recorded before any further run: acceptance is a property of the generated text, which differs between the arms (the greedy path flips at near ties), so one cell cannot separate a numerics effect from a text effect; the acceptance criterion is changed to a pooled one, over four cells: the two above, 64k tokens (the contract's third cell) and a 32k-token cell on the guide text. KEPT needs the mean acceptance difference over the four cells within 0.03 (B not lower than A by more than 0.03 on average) and no cell lower by more than 0.08; the two cells already known sum to -0.076, so the two further cells must average -0.022 or better. If the pooled mean is lower than -0.03 the row is REJECTED. The primary speed criteria are unchanged. Further cells (decode A-B-B-A, `h44-bin`, old routing against the default): 65,536 tokens: tok/s 25.05 -> 27.70 (+10.6%, no overlap), ms/round 78.63 -> 69.50 (-11.6%), acceptance 0.846 -> 0.809 (-0.037); 32,768 tokens on the guide text: tok/s 30.02 -> 32.39 (+7.9%, no overlap), ms/round 68.22 -> 64.27 (-5.8%), acceptance 0.923 -> 0.950 (+0.027). Pooled over the four cells the acceptance difference is -0.0215 (cells -0.063 / -0.013 / -0.037 / +0.027), within the D21 rule; tok/s +6.1% / +11.0% / +10.6% / +7.9% at 8k / 49k / 64k / 32k-guide. Because the routing is default-on wherever `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8` is on (all `--hybrid-moe` runs), a direct text-independent numerics test follows, its criterion fixed before it runs: `llama-perplexity` on 4,096 tokens of `build/pascal/long.txt` with `-b 4 -ub 4` (every decode batch has 4 rows), q8_0 K and V, `-ncmoe 37`, base logits saved with the old routing (`LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=2`) and compared (`--kl-divergence`) with the default routing: KEPT needs a mean KL divergence of at most 0.003 (below the 0.0034-0.02 of the int8 attention approximations already accepted as opt-in) and a same-top-token rate of at least 98.5%; otherwise the routing is made opt-in (`LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS` default 2) and the row is KEPT as opt-in. KL result (`llama-perplexity`, 4,096 tokens of `long.txt`, `-b 4 -ub 4`, `-ncmoe 37`, q8_0 K and V, `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`; base = `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=2`, compared = 4): mean KL divergence 0.0070 +- 0.0003 (median 0.0031, 99% 0.049, max 0.18), same top token 95.2% +- 0.5%, RMS change of the top probability 1.74%, mean ln(PPL ratio) -0.0013 +- 0.0034 (PPL 9.522 against 9.534), correlation of the per-token PPL 99.83%. The criterion (KL at most 0.003 and same top token at least 98.5%) FAILED, so by the rule written before the run the routing is opt-in: the gqa8 kernel quantizes Q to q8_1 like the vector path (the tile kernel keeps Q in f32 and K and V as converted f16), which is the stock behaviour for 1-2 rows but a change for 3-4 rows. The perplexity shows no loss (ratio 0.9988 +- 0.0034), the change is of the size of other opt-in approximations of this tree (int8 QK 0.0034-0.02, delta net quad 0.0115). Final build (`h44d-bin`, default 2): `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` 2,940/2,940; A-B-B-A `H44e` at 8k (default against `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=4`): the default arm has the acceptance (0.868) and hit rate (0.6085) of the old-routing arms of the earlier cells, the opt-in arm 0.805 as before, tok/s 32.56 -> 33.37 (+2.5%, session spread; +6.1% in the first 8k session). Speed with the opt-in (earlier cells): decode tok/s +6.1% / +7.9% / +11.0% / +10.6% at 8k / 32k (guide text) / 49k / 64k, ms per round -5.7% / -5.8% / -8.5% / -11.6%. Advice: add `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=4` (it needs `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`, which `--hybrid-moe` sets by default and plain runs do not) for sessions that go deep; text changes (near ties flip). Limits: one machine, four cells with one text each, a perplexity test at 4k context with 4-row batches (the hybrid path itself was not run through it). SUPERSEDED BY H48 for the default: the KL bar used here was below the floor of the test (H47), the floor-referenced check on two other texts passed, and the default became 4; the final build (`h48b-bin`) passes `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` 2,940/2,940 and the default arm of an 8k A-B-B-A (against explicit `MAXROWS=2`) has the acceptance 0.805 and hit rate 0.6566 of the earlier opt-in arms, tok/s 32.39 -> 33.49 (+3.4%), ms/round 63.75 -> 61.65 (-3.3%), no overlap. |
| H38 | Does p-min 0.9 add on top of the MTP-experts flag? H36 found the flag alone 0.0% at 64k and +4.3 / +5.6% at 32k on two texts; the combination gave +4.7% at 64k (H34a) and +7.2% at 32k on the base text (H32c), and p-min 0.9 alone is text-dependent (H35), so the recipe 'both' has never been compared with the flag alone | +1.5% over the flag alone | flag, harness only | REJECTED by its criteria (mean -0.1%, code cell -2.3% without overlap) | Contract. Outcome: tok/s and ms-per-round ratio of (flag + p-min 0.9) against (flag + p-min 0.7), A-B-B-A, 256 decode tokens, three cells: (a) 65,536 tokens on the default deep text, (b) 32,768 on `build/pascal/h35-guide.txt`, (c) 32,768 on `build/pascal/h35-code.txt`. Criteria, fixed now: p-min 0.9 is KEPT as part of the deep recipe (with the flag) if the mean tok/s ratio over the three cells is at least +1.5% AND no cell loses more than 2% without A/B overlap; otherwise the recipe is the flag with p-min 0.7 and the guide says so. ms per round is reported for every cell. Claims allowed: ratios for these three cells on this machine. Commands: `tools/pascal/abba.sh` with `--pp "" --chats 0 --deep D --tg 256 --hlog [--text FILE]`, after the sibling's H37 session (GPU.lock), script `build/pascal/b9a32b-h38.sh`. Result (A-B-B-A, both arms with the MTP experts on the GPU, p-min 0.7 against 0.9): (a) 64k, default text: tok/s 25.33 / 26.31 / 26.16 / 25.33, mean 25.33 -> 26.23 (+3.6%, no overlap), ms per round 76.57 -> 63.78 (-16.7%), acceptance 0.810 -> 0.928, the text is the same in both arms; (b) 32k, guide text: 31.23 / 30.94 / 30.90 / 31.53, mean 31.38 -> 30.92 (-1.5%, no overlap), ms per round -15.9%, acceptance 0.935 -> 0.972; (c) 32k, code text: 32.47 / 31.91 / 31.50 / 32.41, mean 32.44 -> 31.70 (-2.3%, no overlap), ms per round -19.0%, acceptance 0.887 -> 0.949; peak VRAM 7,321-7,357 MiB in all arms. Mean ratio -0.1% (the criterion was +1.5%) and the code cell loses more than 2% without overlap: REJECTED. Reading. With p-min 0.9 the rounds are 16-19% shorter in every cell and the acceptance rises, but the drafts carry fewer tokens, so tok/s follows the text: +3.6% on the one text that gained at 64k and 32k, -1.5% / -2.3% on the other two. Together with H35 this closes the p-min question on this machine: 0.7 is the value at every depth and text measured, and the only thing that gained at depth on all but the 64k cell is the MTP-experts flag. Claims allowed: the three cells. |

Result (A-B-B-A, `--pp 131072`, `build/pascal/long.txt`, the variable off against on): 258.00 / 311.10 / 311.00 / 253.90 tok/s, mean 255.95 -> 311.05 (+21.5%), no overlap, peak VRAM 7,327 / 7,335 / 7,335 / 7,327 MiB (+8 MiB), hit rate 0.2484 against 0.2465, cache 40 -> 51 slots in all arms. The A arms differ by 1.6% (258.0 against 253.9), the B arms by 0.03%. The mean is above the +20.0% bar and the earlier single session (+22.2%, 254.1 -> 310.6) is reproduced to 0.7 points: CONFIRMED. | H43 | A q4_0 K and V cache for deep decode (guide section 5, third idea): it frees about 0.5 to 1 GB for the expert cache (7 to 13 slots), expected +1 to 2% decode; the q8_0 cache is the owner's setting | +1..2% decode at 64k | flag, harness only | REJECTED (-28.9% tok/s at 64k) | Contract. Outcome: tok/s and ms-per-round ratio of `-ctk q4_0 -ctv q4_0` (the draft cache stays q8_0) against `-ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0`, A-B-B-A, both with `--spec-draft-p-min 0.7`, `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_THREADS=6`, `-fitt 512` and the MTP experts on the GPU (the deep advice of H36 / H38), 65,536 tokens of depth, 256 decode tokens, `--pp "" --chats 0 --deep 65536 --tg 256 --hlog`. Criteria, fixed now: the q4_0 cache is a candidate for deep contexts only if the mean tok/s ratio is at least +2.0% with no A/B overlap; if it is, the paired log-likelihood check (`tools/pascal/ftk_arm.py --ppl`, 144 positions or more) must show a mean dNLL no worse than +0.05 nats for q4_0 against q8_0 before the guide recommends it; below +2.0% the idea is REJECTED as not worth the cache quality, with no quality run. The number of cache slots (start and end) is reported beside the ratio. Claims allowed: speed at 64k on this text; no quality claim unless the second criterion ran. Commands: `tools/pascal/abba.sh` with `ARGS_B="$S7 $MTP --drop \"-ctk q8_0\" --drop \"-ctv q8_0\" --add \"-ctk q4_0 -ctv q4_0\""`, script `build/pascal/b9a32b-h42.sh` after H42. Result (A-B-B-A, 65,536 tokens of depth, 256 tokens, both arms with p-min 0.7, 6 threads, `-fitt 512` and the MTP experts on the GPU): tok/s 25.44 / 18.16 / 18.03 / 25.43, mean 25.44 -> 18.09 (-28.9%, no overlap), ms per round 76.25 -> 109.67 (+43.8%), acceptance 0.810 -> 0.830, cache 34 -> 45 slots (q8_0) against 34 -> 46 (q4_0), hit rate 0.516 -> 0.551, peak VRAM 7,321 / 7,323 MiB, prefill rate of the 64k fill +1.6%. The criterion was at least +2.0%: REJECTED, no quality run (the second criterion applies only to a speed pass). The expected 7 to 13 extra cache slots did not appear: one slot more at the end of the run (the q8_0 cache is committed lazily, so the saving at 64k tokens is small), and a q4_0 K and V cache makes the decode rounds 44% longer, most likely in the multi-row verify attention that converts K and V (the sibling's H40 finding), which was not isolated here. Claims allowed: this cell. |

H44 was measured on the stack without the flag (+6.1..+11.0% at 8k..64k); the flag also shortens the verify (the draft step), so the gains may overlap | +5% or more at 64k with the flag | flag, harness only | KEPT (stacks: +14.3% at 64k, +6.6% at 32k code) | Contract. | H46 | An exact packed attention kernel for the 3-4 row verify batches (the stop hook asked for a measurement of what H44's notes only estimated): the packed GQA kernel with Q kept in f32 and K dequantized in f32 (`kernel<true>`), no q8_1 quantization of Q and no f16 conversion pass; estimated at ~10 ms per 3-4-row round at 49k keys against 27 ms for the tile route and ~5 ms for the dp4a kernel | most of H44's +6..11% decode without its KL 0.007 | code, medium | KEPT as opt-in (`LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_EXACT_ROWS=3`; (b) failed against the original bar, which H47 shows no change can meet) | Contract. (a) Numerics: `test-backend-ops` FLASH_ATTN_EXT passes 2,940/2,940 with `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1 LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=4 LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_EXACT_ROWS=3`. (b) KL: the H44 test unchanged (`llama-perplexity`, 4,096 tokens of `long.txt`, `-b 4 -ub 4`, `-ncmoe 37`, q8_0 K and V; base = old routing `MAXROWS=2`): mean KL divergence at most 0.003 and same top token at least 98.5%. (c) Speed: decode A-B-B-A at 49,152 tokens (A old routing, B exact), 256 tokens, the stack: ms per round at least 6% lower and tok/s at least +3% with no overlap; at 8,192 tokens tok/s not more than 1% below A. Verdict: KEPT as the default for 3-4 rows if (a), (b) and (c) pass, then confirmed by one more cell each at 64k and on the 32k guide text without loss; KEPT as opt-in if (a) and (c) pass but (b) fails; REJECTED if (c) fails (H44's dp4a opt-in stays the faster choice) or (a) fails. Claims allowed: ratios at these depths for one deep prompt, KL on 4,096 tokens, this machine. After the sibling's H45 (GPU.lock); no build before its marker. Result (build `h46-bin`): (a) `FLASH_ATTN_EXT` 2,940/2,940 with the three variables set. (b) KL against the old routing (`llama-perplexity` as in H44): mean KL divergence 0.0062 +- 0.0002, same top token 95.4% +- 0.5%, perplexity ratio 0.9996 +- 0.0033: FAILED the bar (0.003 / 98.5%), but see H47: the exact kernel is no closer to the tile route than the dp4a kernel was (0.0070 / 95.2%). (c) Decode A-B-B-A, A the old routing, B exact: 49,152 tokens tok/s 26.20 -> 27.62 (+5.4%, no overlap: A 26.02-26.37, B 27.59-27.64), ms/round 75.76 -> 68.67 (-9.4%), acceptance 0.814 -> 0.823; 8,192 tokens tok/s 32.16 -> 32.41 (+0.8%), ms/round 64.21 -> 65.83 (+2.5%, slower than the tile route at this depth), acceptance 0.868 -> 0.829. So (c) passes (49k bar met, 8k not more than 1% below) but the exact kernel is not faster than the dp4a kernel: equal per round at 49k (-9.4% against -8.5%), 8 points worse at 8k (+2.5% against -5.7%). By the contract: KEPT as opt-in. Value: a reference with no Q quantization (used in H47), not a speed route. |
| H47 | What is the noise floor of the KL test used in H44 / H46? H44 (dp4a kernel) gave KL 0.0070 and the exact f32-Q kernel of H46 0.0062 against the tile route, almost the same although the exact kernel has no Q quantization; the activations of the quantized matmuls are re-quantized at every layer, which amplifies any arithmetic difference to one floor (AGENTS.md, G33-G36 notes), so the bar of 0.003 chosen in H44 may lie below the floor of ANY change | explains whether the 0.0062 / 0.0070 is a real difference or the floor | measurement | MEASURED (the hypothesis held: floor 0.0057 / 96% same top on prose) | Contract. Measurement only (no performance claim). `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_EPS=e` scales the softmax scale of the packed kernel by 1+e (host side, default 0). Same test as H44 / H46 (`llama-perplexity`, 4,096 tokens of `long.txt`, `-b 4 -ub 4`, `-ncmoe 37`, q8_0 K and V, `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`, `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=4`). Base = the exact kernel (`LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_EXACT_ROWS=3`, e = 0). Compared: (1) the exact kernel with e = 1e-6 (a relative change of every score of one millionth, far below any rounding of the data); (2) the dp4a kernel (no EXACT_ROWS). Hypothesis, fixed now: (1) gives a mean KL divergence of at least 0.004 (two thirds of H46's 0.0062), i.e. the floor of the test is as high as the differences measured; if (1) is at most 0.001 the 0.006 of H46 is a real difference between the tile route and the packed kernels, to be explained before any use. Reading rule: a KL bar can only be set above the floor; (2) tells how much the Q quantization adds to the floor. Claims allowed: KL values on these 4,096 tokens on this machine. After the H46 chain (GPU.lock). Result (`llama-perplexity` as above, base = exact kernel): exact with e = 1e-6: mean KL divergence 0.0057 +- 0.0002, same top token 95.9% +- 0.4%, RMS change of the top probability 1.56%, perplexity ratio 1.0004 +- 0.0029; the dp4a kernel: 0.0062 +- 0.0002, same top token 95.6% +- 0.5%, RMS 1.63%, ratio 0.9993 +- 0.0030. Hypothesis (1) HOLDS (0.0057 >= 0.004): a relative change of every attention score of one millionth, far below any rounding of the data, already gives the KL and same-top figures that H44 (0.0070 / 95.2%) and H46 (0.0062 / 95.4%) measured, so the floor of this test is KL 0.0057 and same top 96% and the bars of 0.003 / 98.5% were below the floor of ANY change, however exact; the Q quantization of the dp4a kernel adds 0.0005 (0.0062 against 0.0057), the tile route against the exact kernel 0.0062 (H46). The floor comes from the re-quantization of the activations in every quantized matmul (a rounding flips somewhere and the flip propagates), which makes the test blind to differences smaller than itself; the perplexity ratio (1.000 +- 0.003 in every case) is the quantity that stays informative. Limits: one text (4,096 tokens), one machine. |
| H48 | Re-evaluate the numerics of H44's dp4a routing against the measured floor of the KL test (H47), on two texts the floor and the candidate have not seen: the original bar (KL 0.003, same top 98.5%) was unattainable by any change, so H44's 'opt-in' verdict rests on an invalid bar | decides whether the +6..11% decode of H44 can be the default of `--hybrid-moe` (where `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8` is already on) | measurement + default | PASSED (both texts; `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS` default is now 4) | Contract (decision D24: a criterion change made after the H44 / H46 results, recorded before the new measurement, with its reason: the original bar was shown unattainable, so a bar referenced to the measured floor replaces it, and it is applied to NEW data). Per text (`build/pascal/h35-guide.txt` and `build/pascal/h35-code.txt`, the first 4,096 tokens, `llama-perplexity -b 4 -ub 4 -ncmoe 37`, q8_0 K and V, `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`): base = the exact kernel; compared: F = exact with e = 1e-6 (the floor), D = the dp4a kernel (`MAXROWS=4`), T = the tile route (`MAXROWS=2`, the current default). PASS for a text if D has a mean KL divergence of at most 1.5 x F and a same-top-token rate of at least F's minus 2 points, and a perplexity ratio within its own error of 1. The row is PASSED if both texts pass; then the default of `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS` becomes 4 (the dp4a kernel; the exact one stays an opt-in reference), the confirmed speed cells of H44 stand, and the docs say so; otherwise the opt-in verdict of H44 stands. T is reported as well (its distance from the exact kernel, for the record). Claims allowed: KL and same-top figures on 2 x 4,096 tokens, the speed cells of H44. Command: `build/pascal/slowprefill-h48.sh`. Result (base = exact kernel; F = exact with e = 1e-6; D = dp4a kernel; T = tile route; mean KL divergence / same top token / perplexity ratio against the base): guide text F 0.00559 +- 0.00118 / 96.24% / 0.9997 +- 0.0024; D 0.00549 +- 0.00087 / 95.75% / 1.0020 +- 0.0025; T 0.00599 +- 0.00085 / 95.51% / 1.0042 +- 0.0027. Code text F 0.00196 +- 0.00018 / 98.39% / 1.0000 +- 0.0014; D 0.00198 +- 0.00015 / 98.39% / 1.0003 +- 0.0015; T 0.00175 +- 0.00012 / 98.73% / 0.9996 +- 0.0015. D is within 1.5 x F on both texts (0.98 x and 1.01 x), its same-top rate within 0.5 points of F's, its perplexity ratio within one error of 1: PASSED on both. The floor itself depends on the text (0.0056 on prose, 0.0020 on code), so a fixed KL bar would have been wrong in both directions: 0.003 fails prose at any exactness and the original 98.5% same-top bar fails the code text for the floor run itself (98.39%). T, the tile route, is as far from the exact kernel as D is (0.0060 / 0.0018 against 0.0055 / 0.0020): neither route is closer to the exact computation than the other within the resolution. Consequence: the default of `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS` is 4 (3-4 row verify batches use the packed kernel wherever `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8=1`, i.e. under `--hybrid-moe`; plain runs without that variable are untouched), `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_MAXROWS=2` restores the tile route, `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_EXACT_ROWS=3` selects the exact kernel. |
| H49 | Is the int8 attention of the layer-major prefill (opt-in: int8 QK with `--lm-prefill-attn-int8`, int8 PV with `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV=1`; +4% at 16k to +22% at 131k prefill) within the noise floor of the first-token KL? Its opt-in verdict (H28) rested on first-token KL figures of 0.002-0.02 and 'not bit-identical', judged without a floor | the largest prefill gain still held back, if the floor-referenced bar (D24) passes | measurement, then a default | PASSED (both arms; `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV` becomes default under `--hybrid-moe`, see D27) | Contract. Knob: `LLAMA_FATTN_EPS=e` (fattn-common.cuh, `launch_fattn`) scales the softmax scale by 1+e, a relative change of every attention score (measurement only, default 0). Harness: `tools/pascal/ftk_arm.py` (plain-decode server, `--lm-prefill`, q8_0 KV, no context checkpoints, greedy, n_probs 20), nine prompts: 4,096 / 8,192 / 16,384 tokens at the text offsets 0 / 100,000 / 200,000 of `build/ctx100k/base.txt` (the contract first said 0 / 150,000 / 300,000; the text has only 300,682 characters, so an offset of 300,000 leaves no prompt: corrected before any run, the bar is unchanged). Arms: R the reference (exact tile attention, no flag); F the floor (R with `LLAMA_FATTN_EPS=0.000001`); Q int8 QK (`--lm-prefill-attn-int8`); P int8 QK + int8 PV (`--lm-prefill-attn-int8` and `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV=1`). Per arm: the first-token KL divergence against R (top-20 renormalised over the union, as `ftk_arm.py --compare`), its mean and standard error over the nine prompts, and the number of prompts with the same top token as R. Bar fixed now (floor-referenced, as in D24, with the standard error of a nine-sample mean in it): an arm PASSES if its mean KL is at most max(1.5 x KL_F, KL_F + 2 x SE_F) and its same-top count is at least F's minus 1. Verdicts: Q and P both pass -> `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV` becomes default on whenever `--lm-prefill-attn-int8` is given (opt-out `=0`), and the first-token record plus the 290-position log-likelihood of H28 stand as the evidence; only Q passes -> the PV stays opt-in; neither -> unchanged, and the figures are reported. If F itself is below 0.0005 (the floor is not a floor for prefill) the bar is the absolute one of H28 and the row says so. Claims allowed: first-token KL on nine prompts of one text, this machine. Command: `build/pascal/slowprefill-h49.sh`, which runs, with `H=build/pascal/h49-bin` (the build with the knob) and `OFF="--offsets 0,100000,200000"`: `python3 -I tools/pascal/ftk_arm.py --bin $H --label h49-R $OFF`; `... --label h49-F $OFF --env LLAMA_FATTN_EPS=0.000001`; `... --label h49-Q $OFF --add=--lm-prefill-attn-int8`; `... --label h49-P $OFF --add=--lm-prefill-attn-int8 --env LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV=1`; then `python3 -I tools/pascal/ftk_arm.py --compare h49-R h49-F h49-Q h49-P`. The knob, exactly: in `launch_fattn` (fattn-common.cuh) after the op's scale is read, `scale *= 1.0f + eps` with `eps = atof(getenv("LLAMA_FATTN_EPS"))` (read once, default 0); it perturbs the softmax scale of every flash-attention op that goes through `launch_fattn` (tile, vector, MMA kernels), not the packed GQA kernel (that has `LLAMA_FATTN_GQA8_EPS` in `ggml_cuda_flash_attn_ext_gqa8`). A kernel of another family (for example the gated delta net) needs its own knob at its own scale or input. Result (build `h49-bin`, nine prompts; first-token KL divergence against R, per prompt in the order 4k / 8k / 16k at offsets 0, 100,000, 200,000): F (floor, e = 1e-6) 0.00547 / 0.00254 / 0.04066 / 0.31740 / 0.14014 / 0.00005 / 0.00005 / 0.00308 / 0.00047, mean 0.05665, standard error 0.03598; Q (int8 QK) 0.00265 / 0.00169 / 0.03477 / 1.05829 / 0.04350 / 0.00005 / 0.00001 / 0.00162 / 0.00190, mean 0.12716 (SE 0.11652); P (int8 QK + int8 PV) 0.00330 / 0.01358 / 0.04087 / 0.69436 / 0.08922 / 0.00005 / 0.00001 / 0.00133 / 0.00194, mean 0.09385 (SE 0.07571). The same top token as R in 9 of 9 prompts in every arm. Bar (fixed before the run) = max(1.5 x 0.05665, 0.05665 + 2 x 0.03598) = 0.12861: Q 0.12716 PASSES narrowly, P 0.09385 PASSES; same-top counts 9 = 9. Reading, with its weakness: the statistic is dominated by one knife-edge prompt (offset 100,000, 4k tokens), where a one-millionth perturbation alone gives KL 0.317 and int8 QK gives 1.058 (top-1 log-probability shift 0.082, same token), so the standard error is as large as the mean and the pass of Q is a narrow one by the bar as written. Robust summaries agree: medians 0.0019 (Q) / 0.0033 (P) against 0.0031 (F); without the knife-edge prompt the means are 0.0108 (Q) and 0.0188 (P) against 0.0241 (F). Supporting evidence that does not depend on the KL statistic: the paired log-likelihood of H28 over 290 positions (int8 QK -0.0098 +- 0.0229, with the PV -0.0619 +- 0.0388 nats) and the 131k / 98k first-token checks. Not shown by this row: the int8 PV on the dense Qwen3.8 27B (its continuation rule failed once for int8 QK with the bf16 slab), which is why the default is scoped to `--hybrid-moe`, the model class measured here. |
| H52 | The int8 PV on the dense Qwen3.8-27B (open since H49: its default is scoped to `--hybrid-moe` because the dense model failed a continuation rule once for int8 QK with the bf16 slab and was never measured with the PV): is int8 QK / int8 QK + PV within the first-token KL floor on that model, 24 prompts? | a kernel-level default for the int8 PV if both models pass (simpler than a hybrid-only switch) | measurement | PASSED (both arms; the default of the int8 PV moves into the kernel, validation of that change in H52b) | Contract. Verdicts: Q and P pass -> the default of the int8 PV moves into the kernel (on whenever the int8 QK flag is on and the cache is q8_0 with head size 256; `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV=0` opts out) and the hybrid-only line is dropped; only Q passes or neither -> the hybrid-only scope stays and the figures are reported. Result (build `h49b-bin`, 24 prompts of 1.5-4.3k tokens; first-token KL against R, mean +- SE, median, same top token as R): F (floor, e = 1e-6) 0.00613 +- 0.00143, median 0.00459, 23/24; Q (int8 QK) 0.00828 +- 0.00120, median 0.00685, 22/24; P (int8 QK + PV) 0.00815 +- 0.00251, median 0.00407, 24/24. Bar = max(1.5 x 0.00613, 0.00613 + 2 x 0.00143) = 0.00920; Q is 1.35 x F, P 1.33 x F: both PASS, top-1 counts within F's minus 2 (21). Without the single worst prompt the means are 0.00770 (Q) and 0.00608 (P) against 0.00500 (F). | H55 | A smaller target ubatch gives the expert cache more VRAM: the decode server keeps the scheduler's compute buffer of the target at its worst case for `-ub 512` (717 MiB in the logs of H53, 265 MiB more for the draft context) while a decode step has at most 4 rows; one cache slot (one expert in each of 40 layers) is 74.5 MiB, so a smaller `-ub` should buy a few slots | +1-3% decode if the hit rate follows the slots | flag | KEPT as advice (a flag: `-ub 128`) | Contract. Otherwise REJECTED with the numbers (a gain under 2% is reported as such). Claims allowed: ratios for this text and depth on this machine. If B passes, `-ub 256` is added as a second arm in a follow-up row. Command: `build/pascal/slowprefill-h55.sh`, queued behind the sibling's H54. | H50 | Stream-k scheduling for the K-quant expert tiles (guide section 5): how much of the expert matmul time is left in wave tails after the flat tile list (H37)? A bound from a profile, before any code | at most a few % of prefill | measurement only | REJECTED (bound 0.28% of kernel time) | Contract. Outcome: from one nsys profile of a 4,096-token prefill on the newest build (`build/pascal/h48b-bin`, the stack, `--pp 4096 --chats 0 --deep 0`), for every `mul_mat_q` launch of the expert matmuls (grid z = 1 with the flat list, or z > 1 without) the grid blocks B, the resident blocks per SM R_sm = min(65,536 / (regs x threads), 2,048 / threads, 32, 98,304 / (static + dynamic shared)) and the card's 15 SMs; waves = B / (15 x R_sm); the tail loss of a launch is (ceil(waves) - waves) / ceil(waves) of its duration, an upper bound of what any dynamic scheduling (stream-k) could recover when all blocks take equal time. Criteria, fixed now: the duration-weighted tail loss over all expert `mul_mat_q` launches, and the share of the whole kernel time those launches take, are reported; if the weighted loss is below 5% of their duration (so below roughly 2% of prefill) stream-k is REJECTED as bounded and no code is written; if it is 5% or more, a prototype contract is written as a separate row. Claims allowed: the bound for this shape (4,096 tokens, ubatch 2,048), nothing about gains. Commands: `tools/pascal/moe_bench.py --wrap "nsys profile ..."` as in H40, then a read-only sqlite query (`build/pascal/b9a32b-h50.py`), one GPU session of a few minutes after H49's build is not needed. Result (profile `build/pascal/h50/pf.sqlite`, `h48b-bin`, 4,096 tokens in 8.16 s = 501.9 tok/s, 9,496 ms of kernel time, 15 SMs, the analysis script is `build/pascal/b9a32b-h50.py`): the K-quant expert `mul_mat_q` launches (Q4_K 1,920 launches 1,454 ms, Q5_K 888 launches 889 ms, Q6_K 72 launches 78 ms) take 2,422 ms = 25.5% of the kernel time; their waves per launch are 34 to 546 (Q4_K gate / up main launch 68 waves, 4 resident blocks per SM; Q5_K 546 waves at 2 per SM), the tail loss bound is 1.11% of their duration = 0.28% of the kernel time. The non-expert Q8_0 launches (1,696 ms, 17.9%) have a bound of 4.69% of themselves = 0.84% of the kernel time, outside the question. The criterion was 5% of the launches' duration: REJECTED as bounded, no code. Limits of the bound: it takes all blocks as equally long and the flat-list grid as launched (an upper bound of 512 tile slots per x column, of which the blocks beyond the real tile count return at once), so a real tail can only be smaller in blocks and is still at most one wave in 34 to 546. |
| H51 | The 4-lane gated delta net prefill kernel (`GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD=1`, opt-in since H21; +4.9% / +6.3% prefill at 4k / 16k when measured; KL 0.0115 against the generic kernel, 'not exact') judged against the noise floor of the first-token KL, as H48 / H49 do for attention; guide section 5 lists an 'exact' version as an idea only because the kernel is not the default | +3..5% prefill as a `--hybrid-moe` default | measurement, then a default | PASSED (KL bar, speed gate, operator gate); the 4-lane kernel is the default under `--hybrid-moe` | Contract. Knob (measurement only, default 0): `GGML_CUDA_GDN_EPS=e` in `ggml_cuda_op_gated_delta_net_impl` (gated_delta_net.cu) does `scale *= 1.0f + e` after the scale `1/sqrt(S_v)` is set, a relative change of every output of every gated delta net layer (the analogue of the attention knob of H49: any such change flips roundings and the requantisation of the activations downstream, which is where the floor comes from). Harness and arms: as H49 (`tools/pascal/ftk_arm.py`, nine prompts at `--offsets 0,100000,200000`, greedy, n_probs 20, first-token KL against R over the top-20 renormalised on the union): R the generic kernel (default); F = R with `GGML_CUDA_GDN_EPS=0.000001`; Q = R with `GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD=1`; per arm the mean KL against R with its standard error and the same-top count. Bar, fixed now and the same as H49's: Q PASSES if its mean KL is at most max(1.5 x KL_F, KL_F + 2 x SE_F) and its same-top count is at least F's minus 1; if KL_F is below 0.0005 the floor is not a floor for this kernel and the row says so (then the absolute bar of H21 applies: KL against the generic kernel not above the generic kernel's own variants, 0.0115). Speed gate, only if Q passes: prefill A-B-B-A on the current build, `GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD` unset against `=1`, `--pp 4096` and `--pp 16384`, the stack; the default changes only if the mean ratio is at least +3.0% at 16k with no overlap and not below 0% at 4k. Then `hybrid_setenv_default("GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD", "1")` in `llama-hybrid-api.cpp` (so only under `--hybrid-moe`; stock behaviour unchanged, opt-out `GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD=0`), and `test-backend-ops -o GATED_DELTA_NET` must pass with the quad kernel on. Claims allowed: first-token KL on nine prompts of one text and the two prefill ratios on this machine; 'not distinguishable by this test', not 'equivalent'. Order: after the sibling's H49 build and runs (the build tree is shared); the eps knob is edited and built only after its binary copy exists. Commands: `tools/pascal/ftk_arm.py` lines as in the H49 row, script `build/pascal/b9a32b-h51.sh`. Result. Build `build/pascal/h51-bin` (HEAD plus the eps knob, the sibling's H49 knob compiled in at default 0). First-token KL against R over the nine prompts (offsets 0 / 100,000 / 200,000 of the harness text, 4,096 / 8,192 / 16,384 tokens): F (generic + eps 1e-6) 0.00339 / 0.01098 / 0.02277 / 0.65517 / 0.04368 / 0.00005 / 0.00000 / 0.00071 / 0.00289, mean 0.08218 (SE 0.07179); Q (4-lane kernel) 0.00359 / 0.01361 / 0.01970 / 1.34206 / 0.04355 / 0.00005 / 0.00005 / 0.00124 / 0.00031, mean 0.15824 (SE 0.14805); same top token as R: 9 of 9 for both. Bar = max(1.5 x 0.08218, 0.08218 + 2 x 0.07179) = 0.2258: Q at 0.1582 PASSES. The statistic is dominated by one knife-edge prompt (offset 100,000, 4,096 tokens: F 0.655, Q 1.342, both huge), so beside the bar: medians F 0.00339, Q 0.00359; means without that prompt F 0.01056, Q 0.01026; paired difference Q - F mean 0.076 (SE 0.076). On the other eight prompts Q is indistinguishable from the floor; on the one unstable prompt both are far above it and Q is twice F (not a significant difference: one prompt). The floor is large because any change of the arithmetic flips the same near tie. Speed gate (A-B-B-A, `h51-bin`, the stack, quad unset against `GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD=1`): 4,096 tokens 534.60 / 561.20 / 560.30 / 536.00 tok/s, mean 535.30 -> 560.75 (+4.8%, no overlap); 16,384 tokens 643.10 / 685.10 / 684.10 / 642.40, mean 642.75 -> 684.60 (+6.5%, no overlap); peak VRAM 7,299-7,317 MiB in all arms. Both pass (bar +3.0% at 16k, not below 0% at 4k). The baseline of these arms uses `--lm-prefill-attn-int8` (the harness default), so the gain is on top of the int8 attention. Default and gates, done: `hybrid_setenv_default("GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD", "1")` in `llama-hybrid-api.cpp` (committed inside the sibling's commit e6daa0e, which took the whole file), `test-backend-ops -o GATED_DELTA_NET` 39/39 on CUDA0 with the kernel on and off, and a presence check on the final build: 4,096-token prefill 573.6 tok/s by default against 548.1 with `GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD=0` (+4.7%, two single runs, not an A-B-B-A). |

| H60 | Nothing has been run near the owner's context of 262,000 tokens: the memory gate was checked at 61k (H34) and the longest prefill so far is 150k (H56, peak VRAM 7,341 MiB); the int8 PV table stops there. Does a 250,000-token prefill fit the memory gate with the new defaults, and does the PV gain hold? | gate: no error, peak VRAM under 8,000 MiB; PV about +22..25% | measurement (gate and one unrepeated pair) | GATE NOT CLEAN (14 graceful declines per arm), PV pair +9.9% (below the +18% bar) | Contract. Text: `build/pascal/long250.txt` (long.txt plus repo documents and sources, about 1.3 MB, at least 250,000 tokens; the prompt is cut to 250,000 tokens by the harness). Binary: a side copy `build/pascal/h60-bin` of the current tree (all defaults of D32 / D34: flat tile list, int8 PV and 4-lane kernel on, 3-4 row routing off). Arms, in this order, one server each: A `LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV=0`, B the default; `--pp 250000 --chats 0 --deep 0` with the harness recipe and the stack flags (`-c 262000`, q8_0 cache, `--lm-prefill-attn-int8`). Two arms take about 55 minutes: an A-B pair, not an A-B-B-A (4 arms would be about 105 minutes, over the one-hour limit of D30), so the ratio is a single pair and carries the order effect and the 3-4% arm-to-arm spread seen at 150k. Criteria, fixed now. Gate: each arm exits 0, its server log has no error / abort / illegal / out-of-memory / 'no room' / decline line, and the peak VRAM of the arm stays under 8,000 MiB; a failure of the gate is the finding and is reported with the log lines. Speed: the pair is CONSISTENT with the table if B / A is at least +18.0% (the 150k point was +22.7% with a 3.8% spread of the A arms); below that it is reported as measured and the guide says the gain does not keep growing beyond 150k. Claims allowed: the gate at 250k tokens on this machine and one prefill ratio; no repeated-session claim. Command: `build/pascal/b9a32b-h60.sh`. Result (`build/pascal/H60-A.txt`, `H60-B.txt`, `h60-bin`, 250,000 tokens of `long250.txt`): arm A (`LLAMA_FATTN_TILE_Q8PV=0`) 2,146.97 s = 116.4 tok/s, arm B (default) 1,954.81 s = 127.9 tok/s, B / A = +9.9% (one pair, A first); both exit 0, peak VRAM 7,341 MiB in both, no crash or out-of-memory. The gate as defined is NOT clean: each server log has 14 lines `hybrid-moe: lm-prefill: no room for 1604 MiB + margin 672 MiB: free 2268 ... MiB, expert cache 2 -> 2 slots (min 8)` followed by `lm_acquire_n: ... this batch takes the ordinary path` (arm B: first at 13.0 minutes with 2,268 MiB free, last at 31.1 minutes with 1,652 MiB free against 1,598 needed). That is the memory guard doing its job (loud, graceful, no wrong data), and it means that late in a 250k-token prompt the layer-major pass could not get its memory and the remaining batches ran on the ordinary path, which is slower and does not use the int8 PV kernel of the pass: the 250k rate and the PV ratio are therefore a mix, not the layer-major rate. Speed criterion: +9.9% against the +18% bar: the gain does not keep growing beyond 150k in this configuration, and the cause above (dilution by declined batches) is the likely one, not a measured one. The free memory falls from 2,268 to 1,652 MiB over the prompt as the lazily committed KV grows (about 2.6 GB at 250k tokens). |

H60 showed 14 declines per arm: the pass asks for 1,604 MiB plus a 672 MiB margin; one slot is one expert bank (about 432 MiB) less, so it asks for about 1,172 MiB, which fits while 1,844 MiB are free (the first declines at 2,268 to 2,136 MiB free) but not at the end (1,652 MiB) | fewer declines; prefill rate at 250k above the dilute 128 tok/s | flag, harness only | KEPT as advice beyond 200k tokens (4 declines, +24.1% over arm B; one unpaired arm) | Contract. Arm C = the arm B of H60 (same binary `build/pascal/h60-bin`, same text, same flags) plus `--add "--lm-prefill-slots 1"`; one server, `--pp 250000 --chats 0 --deep 0`; compared with H60's arm B (unpaired, a different time: the comparison carries a 3-4% run-to-run spread seen at 150k). A loud refusal of one slot is a finding and ends the row. Criteria, fixed now: the setting is KEPT as advice for contexts beyond 200k tokens if the number of `no room` declines in C is at most half of B's (7) AND the prefill rate is at least 10% above B's 127.9 tok/s (so at least 140.7 tok/s) AND the peak VRAM is under 8,000 MiB; otherwise it is REJECTED and the figures are reported. Claims allowed: one arm at 250k tokens on this machine. About 35 minutes of GPU. Command: `build/pascal/b9a32b-h61.sh`. Result (`build/pascal/H61-C.txt`, same binary, text and flags as H60's arm B plus `--lm-prefill-slots 1`): 250,000 tokens in 1,575.77 s = 158.7 tok/s, against 127.9 tok/s for arm B (+24.1%) and 116.4 for arm A (+36.3%); `no room` declines 4 against 14; exit 0, peak VRAM 7,345 MiB (7,341 in B). All three criteria met (declines at most 7, rate at least 140.7 tok/s, VRAM under 8,000 MiB): KEPT as advice for contexts beyond about 200k tokens. Limits: one arm compared with an arm of another session (the spread at 150k was 3.8%), one text; the four remaining declines are the end of the prompt, where the free memory falls below what one slot needs (about 1,844 MiB with the margin). The slot count only matters when the KV commit leaves little room: below about 150k tokens the two-slot window overlaps copy and compute and is the faster setting (not re-measured here). |

Worst first-token |dlogprob|: F 0.0566, D 0.0457 (std). The first-token |dlogprob| above 0.07 occurs on prompt 10 for both (F 0.199, D 0.322) and on prompt 13 for F (0.033, below the bar). Claims allowed: equal DQ-2 counts of the floor and of the routing (22 of 32 each) on 32 prompts of one MoE model with speculative decoding on; nothing about the nine unjudged misses. |
