Criterion: the deep decode at a context of at least 180,000 tokens (`prompt_n` of the result) at least +15% over A (it was +29.4% at 32k and +22.6% at 98k), GPU 0 peak within 150 MiB of A's, GPU 1 peak at most 7,990 MiB, no timeout or CUDA error in any log. If it is lower the number is reported with the decode time per round of both arms. Result (`H98a.table`, `b9a32b-h98.sh`, `prompt_n` 190,023): deep decode 18.23 / 18.14 -> 22.34 / 22.39 tok/s = 18.19 -> 22.37 (+23.0%), ms per round 105.1 -> 81.8, hit rate of the main cache 0.417 -> 0.348 (45 slots against 51); GPU 0 peak 7,335 -> 7,323 MiB, GPU 1 peak 7,415 MiB, no error. Found on the way: the prompt processing of that context was SLOWER with the stack, 256.4 -> 159.3 tok/s (x0.62; at 98k it had been unchanged, H78b 1.003, H83b 1.003). The B1 log gives the cause: the layer-major pass was declined twice at deep positions ('no room for 1612 MiB + margin 672 MiB: free 2234 MiB, expert cache 2 -> 2 slots (min 8) ... this batch takes the ordinary path'), 50 MiB short with the expert cache already at its minimum, because `-ot blk.40...=CUDA0` of D61 keeps 432 MiB of the MTP layer's experts on GPU 0 for good and the KV cache of a 190k context is about 2 GiB. The A arms had no decline. So D61's command has a cost beyond ~150k tokens of context that the earlier tests (up to 98k) could not show. |
| H99 | R1 MET (prefill x1.00, decode +17.9%), R2 NOT MET (prefill x0.76) | Remove the H98 prefill regression at 190k tokens. Two single-arm remedies, each against the A numbers of H98 (prefill 256.4 tok/s, decode 18.19 tok/s; the two A arms agree within 0.5%), same text, `--deep 190000`: (R1) the D61 stack without `-ot blk.40...=CUDA0` (the MTP experts back on the host, 432 MiB returned to GPU 0); (R2) D61 as it is with `-fitt 300` instead of 512 (212 MiB less margin kept free). Criterion for a remedy: prompt processing of the deep context at least 0.95 of A's (>= 243 tok/s) and deep decode at least +15% over A (>= 20.9 tok/s), no 'could not make ... MiB' line in the log, GPU 0 never above 8,100 MiB (R2 trades margin for room). The recommended long-context recipe is the cheaper remedy that passes; if R1 passes and R2 does not, the MTP-experts flag is documented as 'not beyond ~150k tokens'. Result (`h99-r1`, `h99-r2`, `b9a32b-h99.sh`, `prompt_n` 190,023; A: prefill 256.4 tok/s, decode 18.19 tok/s): R1 prefill 257.3 tok/s (x1.003), decode 21.44 tok/s (+17.9%), no 'could not make' line, no error, GPU 0 peak 7,335 MiB: MEETS the criterion. R2 prefill 193.5 tok/s (x0.755), decode 23.40 tok/s (+28.6%), GPU 0 peak 7,535 MiB: the pass was still declined ('no room for 1612 MiB + margin 460 MiB: free 2058 -> 2058 MiB', 14 MiB short, twice), so the 212 MiB of margin given up were not enough: FAILS the prefill criterion. The first decline in the H98 B1 log came about 8 minutes into the prompt, between 115k and 156k tokens (the KV cache of the context is the pressure), so the D61 recipe's `-ot` flag is free up to ~100k tokens and costs prefill beyond ~140k. |
| H100 | CRITERION MET (decode +29.1% at 250k, prefill x1.00) | The context limit of the owner's command (`-c 262000`), above everything tested so far (190k): D67's long-context recipe (the tier stack without `-ot`, `--mtp-draft-vocab 131072`, 4 team threads, 40 swaps) against the tuned one-GPU command, one arm each, `--deep 250000 --tg 256 --chats 0 --pp ""` on `build/pascal/long-ctx.txt` (a single arm per side: an A-B-B-A at this length would take four hours). Criterion: both complete without an error line; B's deep decode at least +10% over A's and B's prompt processing at least 0.95 of A's; GPU 0 and GPU 1 peaks as before (GPU 0 within 150 MiB of A's, GPU 1 at most 7,990 MiB). The two A arms of H98 agreed within 0.5%, which is the noise assumed for a single comparison here. Result (`h100-a`, `h100-b`, `b9a32b-h100.sh`, `prompt_n` 250,023): A prompt processing 130.3 tok/s (32 min), decode 14.62 tok/s; B (tier stack, no `-ot`) prompt processing 130.0 tok/s (x0.998), decode 18.88 tok/s (+29.1%); both ran to the end with no timeout or CUDA error; both logs show the pass declined at the deep end of the prompt ('could not make 1612 MiB of device memory', 14 batches in each arm, the one-GPU command too: the KV cache of a 250k context leaves the pass no room in either), so the prefill is equal and slow in both; the prefill at this length is bounded by the ordinary path, a limit of the one-GPU command itself that this branch does not change. Peaks in `build/pascal/H100.gpu*.mem`. Scope: one run per side. |
| H101 | (a) hypothesis not supported, origin unknown; (b) NOT MET at 16k (-3.0% / -2.5% against a 4% bar), +3..6% at 32k | Two things in one row. (a) The origin of the one SIGINT of H95b: hypothesis 'the tool environment signals leftover background processes'. Probe (`sigprobe.sh` in the scratchpad: a bash + python process that logs every INT / TERM / HUP / QUIT / USR1/2 / PIPE it receives, started with `(nohup ... &)` as the chains were): a call whose `timeout` expired, a call that ran past the tool's own timeout and was moved to the background, and that task's completion. Result: no signal in any of them (the probe's log holds only its start line over 2 minutes of these events), so the hypothesis is not supported; the origin stays unknown. (b) The prompt tail: every prompt ends with a 516-token batch (the server's checkpoint rule) that is below `--lm-prefill-min` 1024 and runs on the ordinary path (about 4 s at 16k, all 40 banks copied to the GPU by the stock path). With `--lm-prefill-min 256` that batch takes the layer-major pass (one bank stream, ~2.6 s). Measurement: the `split_check.py` arms with `--add "--lm-prefill-min 256"` (server `-b 16384`, ref = one GPU, peer = the two-GPU prefill), prompts of 16,384 and 32,768 tokens, one arm each, compared with the H94 A-B-B-A numbers of the same configuration (ref 21.36 / 49.34 s, peer 15.00 / 31.73 s; the two A arms and the two B arms there agreed within 3% and 1%). Criterion: prefill time at least 4% lower than the H94 mean for the same arm at 16,384 tokens (and not higher at 32,768), no error, and the continuation of the greedy decode unchanged in at least 3 of its 4 first tokens against the arm without the flag (the tail takes a different path, so equality of the last digits is not expected). Result (b) (`ref-min256.json`, `peer-min256.json`, one arm each, same server flags as H94 plus `--lm-prefill-min 256`): one GPU 16,384 tokens 21.25 -> 20.61 s (-3.0%), 32,768 tokens 48.45 -> 45.55 s (-6.0%); two GPUs 15.01 -> 14.63 s (-2.5%) and 31.54 -> 30.49 s (-3.3%); the first 4 greedy tokens equal in all four, first divergence at tokens 26-28 (the tail takes the pass: different last digits, as expected); no error. The saving is about 0.4-0.7 s at 16k, where the bar was 4% (0.6-0.85 s): the tail is not 4 s of ordinary path but mostly the fixed cost every request pays (the checkpoint state, the first decode step), so the pass saves little, and a single arm against a 1-3% spread cannot call 2.5% a gain. Not adopted as advice; `--lm-prefill-min 256` stays what the memory notes already say about it for short prompts. |
| H102 | CRITERION MET at a controlled expert cache; NOT met as written on the dynamic cache (cause outside the pass, see D71) - prefill x1.4-1.7 below 3k tokens, x1.2 at 4k, x1.7 at 16k; full stack x1.56 at 2k, x1.77 at 16k, decode +21.5% | Layer interleave for the copy-bound passes: the hook's two 'small candidates' (the follower taking the 516-token tail; the follower's duplicate weights) examined first. (1) The tail: the log of H95b shows the last batch of 512 tokens takes 3.3 s at 16k and 4.1 s at 32k on one GPU and on the split server alike, 23% of the split prefill at 16k; the pass saves only 0.4-0.7 s (H101b) because both paths stream all 40 banks (16.9 GiB) through ONE x8 link, 2.6 s; handing the tail to the follower only moves it (it depends on the main pass), and peer-to-peer (`nvidia-smi topo -p2p r`: OK) cannot raise the computing GPU's own ingress. (2) The duplicate weights cost memory only (1.7 GiB of 4.6 GiB peak). What does reduce the floor: each GPU streams only HALF of the banks if the layers alternate between the cards (even layers on GPU 0, odd on GPU 1), the activations hopping between them (4 MiB for 512 tokens, 16 MiB for 2,048), K/V and state of the odd layers going back at the end. Per layer pair the time becomes max(2 x layer compute + hop, one bank stream 66 ms): 512 tokens ~1.3 s instead of ~2.6-3.3 s, 1,024 tokens ~1.3 s instead of 2.6, 2,048 tokens ~1.8 s instead of 2.6-3.0. Built on the follower of H94 (second model on GPU 1, state import for p0 > 0, per-layer K/V and state copies); new: the executor of a layer is chosen by parity, a GPU stages only its own layers' banks, a hop copy of the activation slab between layers, the follower's odd-layer K/V and final state pushed to the leader, the head by the owner of the last layer. A follower failure fails the pass loudly (the leader's state is advanced, a fallback is not possible). Milestones: M3.0 p0 = 0, prompts of 1,500 / 2,500 / 4,000 tokens: bit-identical tokens and top-5 logprobs against the one-GPU pass for the same request order (the bar of H94 (i)); M3.1 the prompt tail and p0 > 0 (state import, or no import when the follower is in sync from the pass before); M3.2 timing. Criterion: M3.0 bit-identical; M3.2 cold prefill A-B-B-A at 2,048 tokens at least x1.25 and at 4,096 tokens not slower than one GPU, the 16,384-token prompt (split + interleaved tail) at least 5% faster than H94's 15.0 s; no error over 20 mixed requests; a failing follower returns an error and the next request works. Kill: M3.0 not bit-identical after the hop and the exchange are shown exact per layer, or x1.25 not reached at 2,048 tokens once wired (the copy floor argument would then be wrong and is recorded as such). RESULT (2026-11-01; `tools/pascal/split_check.py`, `ilv_abba.sh`, `h2d_dual_bench.cu`; raw files under build/pascal/ilv, ilv2, ilvfull.table). AS BUILT: blocks of two layers alternate (leader 0,1,4,5,...; follower 2,3,6,7,..., and the follower holds layer 39 and does the head); the activation rows of a block hop over P2P (`ggml_backend_cuda_peer_copy`); the follower pushes the K/V of its attention layers per layer and the recurrent rows of its delta layers at the end; a pass with p0 > 0 hands the leader's whole sequence state over as the split does. Used for batches of `--lm-prefill-min` .. `--lm-prefill-peer-min` - 1 tokens. FINDINGS ON THE WAY (each measured). (1) The first wiring computed nothing on the follower (the chunk range of the sweep was still the split's) and was wrong; fixed, bit-identical afterwards. (2) With it right the speed-up was only x1.14: the per-layer timeline (`LLAMA_LM_ILV_TRACE=1`) showed a 235 ms cadence per block, i.e. 117 ms to bring a bank to the follower against 69 ms of the copy. A two-GPU copy probe first ruled out a shared host limit (432 MiB banks: one GPU 6.5 GB/s, both at once 6.5 + 6.3 = 12.8 GB/s from plain pinned memory, the same banks, and the registered model mapping). The cause was the follower page-locking each 432 MiB bank itself (`copy_src_reg_churn`, about 80 ms of cudaHostRegister per bank). Fix: `lm_set_peer` re-points the follower's package sources to the leader's tensors (the same bytes, already pinned by the hybrid engine with the portable flag; 120 of 120 equal by memcmp with `LLAMA_LM_PEER_VERIFY=1`; `LLAMA_LM_PEER_OWN_SRC=1` restores the old path). The same change applies to the sequence split (16,384 tokens: 15.0 s recorded in H94, 11.9-12.3 s now; what the other changes of the build contribute to that was not isolated). M3.0/M3.1 BIT IDENTITY. With the default dynamic expert cache the interleave run differs from the one-GPU run in tokens and top-5 logprobs at most sizes (B1 = B2 exactly, A1 = A1b exactly), although every layer output of every pass (FNV hash of 40 layers in 4 passes, `LLAMA_LM_ACT_HASH=1`), the leader's whole sequence state after every pass (hash per MiB, `LLAMA_LM_STATE_HASH=1`) and, with a slot check (`LLAMA_LM_SLOT_VERIFY=1`), the bank bytes in every slot are identical. Cause, measured with `LLAMA_HYBRID_FREE_WATCH_MS`: after the second pass the cache regrows 33 -> 50 slots in the one-GPU run and 33 -> 51 in the interleave run, because the free memory at the second make_room differs by 2 MiB (short by 1223.3 against 1221.3 MiB) and a slot is 67 MiB; the end memory of GPU 0 differs by 78 MiB; a different residency changes the numerics of the decode (the known property of the hybrid cache). At an equal cache the runs are bit-identical (tokens and every top-5 logprob): frozen cache at 1500 / 2048 / 3000 tokens; `--hybrid-moe-slots 16` at 1500 / 2048 / 2500 / 3000 / 4000 / 4096 / 4500 / 6000 / 16384 (9 of 9, also with the final defaults); 6 continuation requests resumed from the host cache (p0 > 0 passes, prompt_n 704-2404); and, after an injected follower failure, the next 4 requests. M3.2 TIMING, A-B-B-A, dynamic cache, cold prefill (best one-GPU arm = the better of the owner's `--lm-prefill-min` and 256; B with min 256, threshold 4096 at that time): 1,500 tokens 7.1 -> 4.4 s (x1.63), 2,048 6.9 -> 4.45 s (x1.55), 2,500 x1.43, 3,000 x1.31, 4,000 x1.15, 4,096 6.95 -> 6.19 s (x1.13), 4,500 x1.13, 6,000 x1.33, 16,384 20.8 -> 12.3 s (x1.68; H94 was 15.0 s, so 17.8% faster). Criteria: x1.25 at 2,048 met (x1.55); not slower at 4,096 met (x1.13); 16,384 at least 5% under 15.0 s met (12.3 s, 11.9 s with the final defaults); 20 mixed requests of 120 .. 16,000 tokens: 0 errors, 20 of 20 answered; an injected follower failure on the tail pass of a request (after its first pass had succeeded) returned HTTP 500 with the loud lines, the server kept running and the next 4 requests (interleave, split, ordinary, interleave) were bit-identical to the reference at the frozen cache. Kill criteria not triggered. THRESHOLDS (D70): interleave against split by batch size, requests of 3,000 / 4,000 / 4,500 / 6,000 tokens (batches 2,484 / 3,484 / 3,984 / 5,484): interleave 5.20 / 6.05 / 6.60 s, split 5.96 / 5.99 / 5.97 / 6.54 s; the interleave forced at 6,000 .. 16,000 tokens: 8.3 .. 20.1 s against 6.6 .. 12.0 s split. With `--lm-prefill-min` 256 the 512-token tail also runs in an interleaved pass (2,048 tokens: 4.45 s against 5.54 s with the default minimum). FULL STACK (tuned one-GPU command against tier at 42 slots + MTP experts on GPU 0 + vocabulary 131072 + the second-GPU prefill at its new defaults, A-B-B-A, `ilvfull.table`): prefill 2,048 tokens 297.2 -> 463.6 tok/s (x1.560), 3,072 444.0 -> 572.7 (x1.290), 4,096 574.3 -> 653.6 (x1.138), 16,384 743.0 -> 1,317.3 (x1.773; H95b: x1.485); decode, 8 chats, mean 36.40 -> 44.24 tok/s (+21.5%; H95b +20.6%), every chat +17% .. +26%; GPU 1 peak 7,773 MiB (limit 7,990), GPU 0 peak 7,355 (A 7,303); no error line in any arm. Other checks: `-np 2` smoke with two simultaneous 2,500-token prompts PASS (24 pass lines, 0 fallbacks); full build 0 errors; ctest 159 of 190 (the 31 failures are 26 model-backed layer-streaming targets without `Q38LS_MODEL_PATH`, 2 Qwen3.8 model-backed, the python modules `jinja2` and the fp8 converter's imports, and the tokenizer-vocabs test that already failed); `test-backend-ops -o MUL_MAT_ID` 2/2 backends. LIMITS: a follower failure inside an interleaved pass fails that request (no fallback is possible: the leader's state is advanced); the interleave needs the same follower memory as the split; the Windows build of the new code was not compiled (no MSVC here). |
| H103 | CLOSED by measurement (2026-11-01): (a) tail pass at the link rate, (b) first pass within 6% of its bound, (c)(d) as stated | Residue of H102. (a) CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT, no build: the tail pass of the interleave runs at the link rate (consecutive own banks are ready 75-76 ms apart; a bank is 486.5 MB = 74.9 ms at the measured 6.5 GB/s, `h2d_dual_bench`), so a third slot or another block size cannot lower it; the state hand-over costs 45-73 ms (export, leader) at the batch sizes served and the import hides behind the first hop, at most 1.5% of a request, not worth the risk of a stale follower. (b) OPEN: the FIRST pass of a 1.5k-3k prompt takes ~2.4 s although its two bounds are ~1.5 s each (copy of 20 banks per GPU; the serialized compute chain of 40 layers at 1,484 tokens: 0.65 + 0.85 s). Prediction: with a 2-slot window a GPU cannot start the copy of bank k+2 before layer k is computed, and the hops serialize the two GPUs, so the copy engine idles part of each cycle; the per-layer timeline (`LLAMA_LM_ILV_TRACE=1`) of that pass shows where. Falsifiable criterion, fixed before the run: the timeline accounts for the excess of the pass over max(copy, chain) by named waits (slot wait, hop wait, hop copy, push), and an experiment that removes the largest named wait (candidates: `--lm-prefill-slots 3` with a slot index by position in the GPU's own layer list instead of `il % n_slots`; block size 4) lowers the cold prefill of a 2,048-token prompt by at least 5% (A-B-B-A, same binary, dynamic cache, three runs agree within 2%) with the output bit-identical at `--hybrid-moe-slots 16`. If the excess is a named wait that no knob here can remove, (b) is closed with that number. (c) DECIDED, not measured: the 2 MiB flip of the cache regrow (D71) is not a speed question and a hysteresis would only move the edge; not pursued. (d) NOT DONE and not doable here: the Windows compile of the new code (no MSVC; installing one is a system change). RESULT of (b), measured from the per-layer timelines of the 1,484-token interleaved pass (`LLAMA_LM_ILV_TRACE=1`, `ilv2/T_peer.server.log`): consecutive banks arrive 76-83 ms apart on both GPUs (the raw copy of a bank, 486.5 MB, is 74.9 ms at 6.5 GB/s), the first bank at 100 ms (leader) and 179 ms (follower), the pass ends at 1.64 s (leader) and 1.72 s (follower) against a bound of 20 x 76.5 ms + 0.1 s = 1.63 s: wall 1.73-1.76 s, i.e. 0.10-0.13 s over the bound, 2.3-2.9% of a 4.45 s request, under the 5% criterion even if removed entirely. The premise that the pass took ~2.4 s came from pre-fix pass lines and was wrong. No knob (slots, block size) can lower the link-rate floor; (b) is closed. The same trace shows where the rest of the request goes (see H104). |
| H104 | CLOSED, criterion (1) NOT met (-3.0% at 2,048 tokens against the 5% bar); exactness met; switches stay default OFF | The fixed cost of a pass request, now the largest single term of a 2,048-token request (4.45 s: passes 1.76 + 1.70 s at the link rate, the other ~1 s). Measured with the resize line made visible (`LLAMA_LM_LOG=1`; `ilv2/R_peer.server.log`), per request: the shrink before the first pass 51 -> 31 slots per layer takes 262-270 ms and UPLOADS 465-495 experts from the host (the refill takes the highest-scoring experts, resident or not; a shrink that uploads nothing, 51 -> 27 slots, takes 128 ms), and the regrow after the last pass 31 -> 51 takes 344-352 ms, of which ~245 ms are the link-bound upload of 800-840 experts (1.6 GB at 6.5 GB/s) that the resize waits for, on the path to the first token. Both are in `moe_hybrid_engine::resize`: it sets the `map` entry of every slot at once and waits for the uploads, whereas a promotion publishes its slot only when its copy event has completed (`pending`, `on_token`). CHANGE (two runtime switches, default decided by the result): (S) `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_SHRINK_KEEP_ONLY`: a shrink keeps the highest-scoring experts among those RESIDENT and uploads nothing (the regrow, which takes the top of the same scores, is unchanged in composition); (G) `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_REGROW_ASYNC`: the uploads of a regrow are queued as one batch after all device-to-device copies, the resize returns without waiting, and the new slots are published through `pending` when the event completes; until then those experts are misses (computed on the host: correct, slower). Only the regrow after a layer-major pass uses (G); start-up sizing keeps the synchronous path. Resize must publish nothing whose copy has not completed: a resize that finds `pending` entries of dropped (never submitted) promotion copies must discard them. CRITERIA, fixed before the run (same binary, A = both switches 0, B = both 1, A-B-B-A, dynamic cache, the peer defaults, cold requests of 2,048 and 3,000 tokens): (1) time to the end of the prompt (the `prompt eval time` of the server) at 2,048 tokens at least 5% lower in B (expected -0.3 s of 4.45 s), the two A and the two B runs each within 2% of each other; (2) exactness: with `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_RESIZE_VERIFY=1` every resize of a 30-request run reads back 0 banks that differ from the host, AND a deliberate stress (a regrow followed at once by a shrink, 20 times) ends with 0 differing banks and no crash; (3) the eight-chat decode of the full stack (`moe_bench`) of B not more than 2% below A (the transient window must not cost the steady state); (4) the text is allowed to differ during the window (labelled text-changing, as H21/H27): report the first-token logprob difference of B against A over the size ladder, no bar. KILL: (1) not reached, or any differing bank, or a crash: the switches stay default OFF (documented, not removed) and the result is recorded as the cause. RESULT (2026-11-01; `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_SHRINK_KEEP_ONLY`, `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_REGROW_ASYNC`, same binary, A = both 0, B = both 1; raw files build/pascal/h104, h104b). CRITERION (2) MET: with `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_RESIZE_VERIFY=1` 50 resizes (24 queued) read back 88,960 slots, 0 banks differ from the host, with a stress of 3 immediate shrink + regrow cycles after every pass and no crash or assert; with the final design (batch handed to the copy thread) `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_PUBLISH_VERIFY=1` read every published slot back at the moment of publication: 6 publications, 5,062 slots, 0 differ. CRITERION (1) NOT MET, A-B-B-A, cold requests, the `prompt eval time` of the server: 2,048 tokens A 4.46 4.29 4.46 4.29 s (mean 4.375), B 4.30 4.19 4.29 4.20 s (mean 4.243): 3.0% lower (first version, resize submitting inline: 3.1%); 3,000 tokens A mean 5.229, B mean 5.143: 1.6% lower; the spreads inside an arm are 2.1-3.8%, so even the gain that exists is at the edge of the noise. Bar 5% (0.22 s); got 0.13 s. WHY (named waits, measured with the phase times of the resize line): the shrink really drops from ~270 ms to ~130 ms (it uploads nothing, 0 of 1,160 slots from the host), but the regrow only moves its cost: the layer loop of the resize takes 95-107 ms (alloc 67-75, device copies queued 7, free 21-24), the submission of the ~2,400 copy regions another ~140 ms on the main thread (~58 us per region from the registered mapping), and the transfer of 1.6 GB 245 ms. Handing the batch to the copy thread (as promotion batches are) cut the call from 245 to 95-110 ms, yet the end-to-end time did not move (3.0%): `on_token` settles the DMA state before it reads the event, so the first decode waits for the remainder of the submission, and the upload runs against that decode. Not tried, and the only way left to the bar: submit the batch in slices over the first tokens, or fewer larger regions (a new-slot assignment in expert order). TEXT: slots are exact when published, but the experts of the window are misses (host numerics): the first token's top-5 logprobs differ by 0.05-0.25 nats from A in 4 of 4 runs and the text differs in 3 of 4 (labelled text-changing; not a bar). Criterion (3), the steady-state decode, was not run: the kill rule applies first. |

## Decision log

- D1 (2026-10-29) Autonomous mode applies (owner command above). Reversible: the owner can stop at any time.
- D2 (2026-10-29) The control binary of this goal is `build/pascal/rem-bin` (the build that includes the remainder-tile code, flag off): changes are compared with it, never with a rebuilt tree.
- D3 (2026-10-29) PR #6 is left as pushed. Work from here is committed locally and not pushed (pushes are never autonomous).
- D4 (2026-10-29) The draft-depth finding (a draft row plus its draft step cost ~20 ms per round; the head and the draft MoE run on the CPU) makes `--spec-draft-p-min` the main lever; H17 is closed without a build.
- D5 (2026-10-29) Flag-only or environment-only results are delivered as documented advice (and in the harness recipes), not as changed code defaults: the owner's command sets these flags itself, and a default that changes the safety margin (`-fitt`) or the speculative policy is the owner's choice.
- D6 (2026-10-29) Trade-offs accepted by decision (K2): the stack costs 510 MiB of peak VRAM (the purpose of `-fitt 512`) and 1.2 to 2.3% of prefill rate (re-uploads in the cache resize, targeted by H10); the TTFT recipe costs 3% on 300 to 500-token turns and 4.7% on a 384-token cold prompt (the 4-token checkpoint chunk is an extra batch). Both are recorded in the guide as advice with the numbers, not as defaults.
- D7 (2026-10-29) The ms-per-round rule of K1 is kept as written for H21; it is rerun with eight chats instead of being loosened.
- D8 (2026-10-29) Defaults changed in code, each meeting the default rule (exact, K1 at two lengths or two sessions, gated or harmless elsewhere): remainder tiles on for NVIDIA GPUs older than Volta (H12; `GGML_CUDA_MMQ_REMAINDER=0` turns them off), and the device-to-device cache resize (H10; `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_RESIZE_KEEP=0` turns it off). Reversible by the two variables. Opt-in only (not exact, so not defaults): `GGML_CUDA_GDN_QUAD=1` (H27), the MTP-experts-on-GPU flag (H21).
- D9 (2026-10-29) Stop decision. The closing profiles (prefill 16k, decode 3 chats) leave five prefill categories at 5% or more: flash attention 21.5%, `mmq` Q4_K 17.4%, Q8_0 17.1%, Q5_K 14.0%, gated delta net 10.4%. Each has a resolved hypothesis (H28, H30, H29, H30, H24/H27). H28-H30 were first resolved as REJECTED by bound, without experiments; see D10, which reopened them.

## Acceptance record (the agent's own, autonomous; not owner approval)

Scope: what the contract defines and nothing more. The claims are speed ratios on this machine, this model and the harness prompts, measured A-B-B-A (or
single arms where the ledger says so); no quality claim beyond bit-identity, the recorded text comparison and one KL / perplexity check of the opt-in delta net kernel;
no claim for other hardware, models or Windows; no claim of fitness for production.

| Criterion | Evidence |
|---|---|
| K1 speed | KEPT items: the flag stack (H1-H3, F1), the resize (H10, `H10`: +6.8% / +3.9%), the remainder tiles (H12, `rem1`), the TTFT recipe (H9, H22, F3), the MTP-experts flag (H21, F4, judged on ms per round), the delta net kernel (H27, `H27` / `H27b`, prefill metric). Each has the session table named in its ledger row. Rejected items did not reach 2% or overlapped their control |
| K2 collateral | H10: decode tok/s +0.4 / +2.5 / +2.7% (`H10b`), peak VRAM unchanged, one cache slot fewer recorded. Stack: 510 MiB and 1 to 2% prefill recorded as D6. TTFT recipe: +3% on 300 to 500-token turns recorded. H27: peak VRAM +64 MiB at 12k recorded, decode no systematic effect |
| K3 exactness | Remainder tiles: 30 comparisons 0 differ. Resize: 0 banks differ in 10 verified resizes. Text-changing, labelled: stack settings that move the cache, H21, H27 (KLD 0.0115 = the size of any reorder of the sums; PPL 18.72 against 18.78) |
| K4 gates | G1 MUL_MAT_ID 887/887 (also matvec rows 1 and 4), MUL_MAT 1,218/1,218, GATED_DELTA_NET 39/39; G2 3,295 checks 0 failures; G3 30 comparisons 0 differ; G5 GPU free before every arm. No error, abort or memory-guard decline in the arms |
| K5 documented | LINUX-PASCAL.md 3.8-3.10 and 4, PARAMETERS.md, currentstate.md 2026-10-29, this ledger |
| Stop condition | Every ledger row is KEPT, REJECTED or PASSED/RESOLVED (H15, the CPU governor, was BLOCKED as a system setting and is closed by a read-only frequency record: the busy cores already run at the maximum clock). Closing profiles taken (`build/pascal/close-pp`, `close-tg`, git-ignored): five prefill categories at 5% or more, each with a resolved hypothesis (D9); H28 (int8 PV, kept as opt-in), H29 (rejected) and H30 (kept) all by experiment (D10, D11) |

Not certified: the MTP-experts flag and the delta net kernel at 32k / 60k prompts; hit-versus-fresh equality with the 4-token checkpoint chunk; any other machine.
- D10 (2026-10-29, later) D9 reopened by the stop hook's feedback that H28-H30 were closed on estimates without experiments. H29 and H30 were then explored with standalone inner-loop microbenchmarks (`tools/pascal/mmq_q8_inner_bench.cu`, `mmq_q4k_inner_bench.cu`) and real tile-configuration builds. H30 turned into a kept change (K-quant expert tiles at 128 threads, occupancy 3: +2.2% / +2.3% prefill, bit-identical); H29 is rejected by experiment. H28 (attention): the one tuning axis of the existing kernel that had never been swept, 64 query columns per block, was built and timed and is 64 to 125% slower; the stop hook then objected a second time that the int8 PV route was deferred, not tested, so it was built (D11). The acceptance below is amended accordingly.
- D11 (2026-10-29, latest) The int8 PV was built and measured instead of being left as a research item (H28). The V cache is q8_0 with a scale per key and 32 head-dim values, which cannot be factored out of an integer dot over keys; the prototype therefore requantizes the V rows of each 32-key tile to one scale per block, transposes them to words of 4 keys and quantizes P per column with dithered rounding. Round-to-nearest P was rejected on evidence (a biased first-token KL of 0.147 on one 32k prompt) and a two-byte P was rejected as no faster than FP32 PV; the kept variant is an opt-in env variable (not exact, so not a default under the default rule), +4% / +9% / +13% prefill at 16k / 32k / 49k with the 64-key tile (A-B-B-A). The acceptance record is amended: H28 is KEPT as opt-in.
- D12 (2026-10-30) The last open items of the int8 PV and the two ledger rows measured only by bounds were run: quality at 131k and a 144-position paired log-likelihood (no loss), the q4_0 cache on the hybrid server (+2.8% / +7.8%), H13 (covered by the H30 sweeps) and H16 (the prototype that applies the routing weights before the down projection gave +0.4..1.0%: rejected, reverted). A second copy of this session (same transcript) worked in the same worktree for a while; the GPU was handed over with a lock file and messages, the sibling's H16 perf numbers were corrected against the profile, and one session at a time committed.
- D13 (2026-10-30) The stop hook's point that 'no open row' records a convention, not the absence of ideas was taken as a prompt to look for untested conditions: the one found was depth (the decode stack and the decode attention kernel had only been measured at 0-16k tokens). H32 tested it by screening and two A-B-B-A sessions: p-min 0.9 is better than 0.7 from 32k tokens of depth (+3.2% tok/s, -14% ms per round), the GQA8 kernel does nothing. Recorded as flag advice, not a default (the owner's command sets the threshold). Remaining untried ideas are listed in the guide as not pursued, each with its expected size.
- D14 (2026-10-30) The last BLOCKED row, H15 (CPU governor), was closed without touching the setting: the question it asks (do the busy cores already run at the maximum clock?) was answered by a read-only record of the clock and utilisation of the 8 logical CPUs during a 4-chat decode (busy cores at 3,700 MHz or more in 98-100% of the points, median 3,898 of 3,900 MHz). A governor A/B would need the system change, which stays outside this work; the record bounds the headroom at 1-2% of busy points, not a measured speed difference. The paired log-likelihood of the int8 attention paths was widened to 290 positions on two stretches of text: no loss is detectable (resolution +-0.05 / +-0.08 nats), 5.5-6.9% of positions change the top token. The int8-QK arm of the second set was interrupted from outside (its server logged 'Received second interrupt'; the sibling session says it sent no signal) and was rerun with the same command; the queued rerun first never started, because its wait loop used a pgrep pattern that matched its own parent shell (found from the process list when the done marker did not appear), and it was launched directly instead.

They hold: the deep-context recipe (p-min 0.9 + MTP experts) is +7.2% at 32k and +4.7% at 64k; the gate at 61k is clean; the 98k int8 PV gain replicates (+19.7%). The sibling's H33 and H35 qualify the p-min half of that recipe (its gain is not monotone in depth and may belong to the text), so the guide now says the MTP-experts flag is the part that held at both depths and p-min 0.9 is neutral to positive from 24k up. No claim goes beyond one deep text, 256 tokens and this machine.

Both rows were REJECTED by criteria written before the first run (not weakened afterwards); the 19-line code was reverted; the deep-context gain that held at 32k on three texts is the MTP-experts flag (H32c / H36; at 64k the flag alone is 0.0%, H36). The p-min advice stays 0.7.

- D43 (2026-10-30) The one idea left on this machine that could be larger than anything in the ledger is excluded by the owner's standing rule, not by cost: the box has a second GTX 1070 (8 GiB, idle), and its memory used as an extra expert cache (about 110 more slots of 74.5 MiB, so about 155 slots against today's 45) would raise the hit rate (0.52 at 45 slots) and with it the decode speed. It is untested and its size is an expectation, not a measurement (the extra cache would be reached over PCIe, which the host-expert path already is not; the cost and the gain are unknown). The owner's instruction for llama-server on this machine is to run on ONE GPU and never touch or split across the second card, lifted only for the separate two-GPU model task; multi-GPU is also out of scope in `AGENTS.md` unless an approved task adds it. It is therefore not run, and it is listed here so that the owner can lift the rule for this model if the decode speed matters more than the single-card constraint.
- D44 (2026-10-30) D43 excluded the second-GPU expert cache by the owner's one-GPU rule without a size; H68 gave it a bound from one card: decode is linear in the miss rate (R squared 0.997), so a perfect cache would reach 69.7 tok/s against 39.6 at 56 slots; a realistic second card (about 155 slots) would be worth an estimated +35 to +47% decode (extrapolated, not measured, PCIe cost not included). That is larger than every other lever in the ledger, so the owner should weigh it: lifting the one-GPU rule for this model would turn the question into a measurement (a second `llama-server` expert cache on GPU 1 needs engine work in `llama-moe-hybrid`, not a flag). The rule is the owner's, set explicitly, so it was not lifted by the agent; the grant's list of agent decisions does not extend to overriding a standing instruction about the owner's hardware.
- D45 (2026-10-30) The second-GPU expert cache of D43 / D44 was bounded without touching the second card: the engine's routing trace replayed through its own policy reproduces the hit rates measured at 14 to 64 slots (within 0.03 at 6 of 6 points) and predicts 0.91 to 0.94 hit rate at 155 slots, i.e. 57 to 60 tok/s against 38 to 40 at today's 45 slots (about +40 to +50% after correcting the replay's optimism of up to 0.027). The cap with a perfect cache is 67 tok/s. The idea is still untested on a second card and still needs the owner's lifting of the one-GPU rule and engine work (a remote expert path with two link crossings per layer). The estimate is now a validated model, not an extrapolation, for short chats.
- D46 (2026-10-30) The policy lever was bounded on one card: the offline-optimal hit rate at 45 slots is 0.84 against the engine's 0.64, so a perfect predictor of routing would be worth 15 ms of a 68 ms round; every online rule held to the promotion budget gets within 0.02 of the engine's rate, and larger budgets lose live. Together with H68 / H69 this puts the remaining decode headroom into two places: more cache slots (another card) and knowing the routing earlier (a predictor), neither a flag and both outside what was authorised here (the first by the owner's one-GPU rule, the second by earlier measurements that capped it).
- D47 (2026-10-30) The routing predictor named in D46 as the one way toward the oracle's hit rate was not built, on evidence already in the record plus link arithmetic: the earlier hidden-state pre-gating study (G13, `tg-beat-stock-8gb-262k-archive.md`, host-only, 81 slots on the 24 GB card) found good recall (0.87 of the true 8 within the predicted 12) and a modelled value of 7 to 13% of the token only with copies treated as free and instantaneous, on a 14.4 GB/s link where one expert took 117 us against a layer period of about 190 us; its own conclusion was that the lead window holds about 1.6 copies. On this card the link is 6.4 GB/s, one expert-layer is 1.9 MiB, so a copy takes about 0.30 ms and two prefetches per layer and token (80 copies, 24 ms of link time per round) would occupy the link for about a third of a 68 ms round on top of the 16 promotions the engine already issues, and an extra promotion was measured (G17c) to cost about 24 us of GPU and link time without shortening the token. Expected (arithmetic, not measured): the benefit is below 5% of a round and the engineering is large (a pre-gate router pass per layer, copies queued ahead of use). It is listed, not run; it is the only remaining route to the 15 ms of H70 on one card.
- D48 (2026-10-30) H71 put a ceiling on the routing predictor of D46 / D47 instead of arithmetic: a perfect next-row oracle limited to 8 to 16 extra copies per row is worth 4.7% to 8.5% of a round on the traces (3.0% to 5.4% at the precision of G13). That is above the 2% line of D38, so the idea was not dropped by the bound; it was not built on a judgement recorded here: the model's copy cost comes from a measurement (G17c) in which more copies did not shorten the token, the live engine lost 1 to 5% with 16 more promotions per token, and the link time of the copies (4.8 ms per row at 16 extra) is of the order of the benefit, so the live value is probably under 2%, while the change touches the GPU graph and the spinning host handshake (the class of defect that produced three field crashes in AGENTS.md). If the owner wants the last few percent of decode on one card, this is the design to build, with H71 as its upper bound; I did not build it because its expected value after the live discount is below the bar of D38 and its risk to correctness is the highest of any item in the ledger.
- D49 (2026-10-31) The owner asked to pull the latest code from GitHub, merge it into this branch, test, and open a pull request. `origin/main` had moved by 16 commits: `llama.cpp` synced to b12004 (2,215 files) and the doc-size guard. The merge (commit af8ddd7) had 5 conflicted files; resolved by hand: `mmq.cuh` (the flat tile list and the remainder tiles ported to upstream's new grid order, fusion parameter and caller-side `J_best`), `mmvq.cu` (the MoE matvec rows knob forwards the new fusion arguments), `llama-moe-hybrid.cpp` (upstream had converted it to CRLF: merged three-way on LF), `test-backend-ops.cpp`, `currentstate.md`. The gates found two interactions that no conflict marker showed, both fixed in commit 667eb91: (1) the shared-memory limit of the two tile variants was set once per kernel from the launching variant's size, so the Pascal 128-thread Q4_K / Q5_K tiles left the 256-thread fallback variant with too little (cudaLaunchKernel invalid argument in MUL_MAT); (2) the int8 tile launch passed its warp size where upstream had inserted `use_sparse` (the 32 became `true` and tripped `GGML_ASSERT(n_kv_max_query > 0)`). Gates on the merged tree (GTX 1070): MUL_MAT 2,522/2,522, MUL_MAT_ID 949/949 (also with the flat list and the remainder tiles off or in modes 2 and 3), GATED_DELTA_NET 46/46 (also with the 4-lane kernel), FLASH_ATTN_EXT 4,060/4,060 in default, GQA8, GQA8 with 4 rows, int8 QK and int8 QK + PV modes, `test-moe-lean-expert` 3,295 checks 0 failures, `test-moe-hybrid-dup-ids` 80/80 on the flagged path (its unflagged negative control now aborts in upstream's new fallback and runs only with `MOE_HYBRID_DUP_BARE=1`), `test-sampling`, `test-grammar-integration`. Real model, pre-merge build against the merged build, A-B-B-A (`MRG1`): prefill 4,096 tokens 569.3 -> 571.8 tok/s (+0.4%), 16,384 tokens 730.8 -> 743.4 (+1.7%); decode (three chats of 128 tokens, 45 to 51 slots) is SLOWER in the merged build: 38.6 -> 35.4 tok/s (-8.5%) over three alternating pairs, deterministic (hit rate 0.6000 against 0.5692 in every pair), -6.4% on chat 0 alone with an identical text and identical draft counts (3,388 ms against 3,619 ms for 128 tokens). Findings so far, none a cause: the CUDA kernel time of a decode run is equal (12.6 s against 12.5 s), the GPU spends 15% longer waiting for the host experts, the lean host leg's stage times are equal or better, switching off CUDA graphs or fusion does not recover it, the engine code differs from the pre-merge branch only in six API-call lines; a replay of the two routing traces through the cache policy gives the same hit rate for both (0.531 and 0.532) but the live engine reaches 0.541 before the merge and 0.487 after it, and the per-step hit rate of the first 20 decode steps is much lower after the merge (0.16 to 0.29 where it was 0.6 to 0.7), while steady-state steps are close: the cache learns slower at the start of a request, and the first decode rows of the merged build carry no routing for the last layer. The 128-token chats are dominated by that start. Not resolved; the PR says so.
- D50 (2026-10-31) The decode slowdown of the merged build (D49) is explained: it is a cold-start effect, and the steady state is equal. (1) The upstream sync alone does not slow decode: `origin/main` built in a scratch worktree and the session-start control build (`ctl-bin`) both decode the three-chat test at 36.0 to 36.2 tok/s with the same flags. (2) `origin/main` crashes with `GGML_ASSERT(n_kv_max_query > 0)` (fattn-common.cuh) whenever `--lm-prefill-attn-int8` is on, on this GPU: the same shifted-argument defect that `667eb91` fixes (the sync re-applied the old call of `launch_fattn`), so the merge also repairs `main`. (3) The pre-merge branch had a head start that the merged build lost: with the server warm-up on, the first shrink of the expert cache (45 -> 28 slots at the first prompt) kept 608 slots and re-uploaded 512 (the engine already had routing statistics and chose the popular experts), while in the merged build it keeps 1,120 and uploads none (no statistics: the first 28 slots by index); `--no-warmup` makes the pre-merge build behave like the merged one (1,120 kept, 0 uploaded). So upstream's warm-up (since the sync it asks every expert, so the hybrid builder declines it) no longer primes the hybrid engine. The effect is the first requests after a start: per chat of the three-chat test merged / pre-merge = 0.90, 0.89 (text differs) and 0.95 for chats 0, 1, 2, and over eight chats of 256 tokens the mean is 37.58 against 37.75 tok/s (-0.5%), with the first chat -4.4% and the rest within +-2%. Not a defect to fix: the priming was an accident of BOS/EOS routing; a real prior is the existing `--hybrid-moe-freq` table (`LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_FREQ`), which this run did not use. The PR description was corrected.
- D51 (2026-10-31) The named hypothesis of D50 was tested: a held-out expert frequency prior (`--hybrid-moe-freq`, built by the new `tools/pascal/make_freq_prior.py` from routing traces) recovers most of the cold-start loss of the merged build (+4.4% mean, +13.7% on the first chat, hit rate 0.569 -> 0.607). It is advice, not a default: the right table depends on the workload, and a wrong one is no worse than the uniform prior only by assumption (not measured). The recipe is in the guide and PARAMETERS.
- D52 (2026-10-31) H73 bounded the risk of the expert prior of D51: a table from another workload (code at depth) gives +5.4% on the cold-start chats, the adversarial (inverted) table -1.4%, all of it in the first chat. A prior is therefore advice with a small downside; whether it should be a default (a generic table shipped with the model, built from mixed traffic) is not decided here, because the table depends on the model file and the quant and would have to be generated and shipped per model.
- D53 (2026-10-31) The branch had no CI, so the whole local suite was run on the merged tree (GTX 1070, `build/llama-cuda`, every target built). The full build needed one fix that is not from this work: `tools/layer-streaming/main.cpp` uses `INT_MAX` without `<climits>`, which GCC rejects (the runner had never been built here; `origin/main` has the same gap); the include was added. `ctest` over all 190 tests: 156 passed, 34 failed, none of them in the code of this branch: 2 Python tests (`jinja2` and a conversion dependency not installed), `test-download-model` (network), `test-tokenizers-ggml-vocabs` (a vocabulary file, `ggml-vocab-gpt-oss.gguf`, present on disk but not loadable and not tracked in the repository), and 30 model-backed `layer-streaming-*` / `qwen38-*` tests that refuse to run without `Q38LS_MODEL_PATH`. With that variable set to the dense Qwen3.8-27B model, 118 of the 119 model-backed tests pass; the one left, `layer-streaming-host2-preflight`, needs the Qwen3.6 MoE model and then refuses because its bounded configuration (6.5 GB + 0.5 GB reserve) does not fit the 6.39 GB of free VRAM of an 8 GB card, the capacity refusal working as designed (that test was written for a 24 GB card). Not run: the Python tests (modules missing), the download test (network).
- D54 (2026-10-31) The owner lifted the one-GPU rule for one experiment: asked whether GPU 1 may be used to test the extra expert-cache tier (the idea of D43-D45), the answer was 'Yes, for this experiment only'. Scope as recorded: opt-in code, the default stays one GPU, every other run keeps the pin to GPU 0 (`CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`); GPU 1 is used only by the programs of rows H74 and its successors, one at a time, and never while another compute process is on either card. The experiment is staged so that each step can end it: H74 measures the latency of a GPU 1 request (this row), a trace-driven model of the decode with a GPU 1 tier follows if the latency allows it, and an integrated prototype only if the model still shows a gain. The hazards that `AGENTS.md` records for the GPU-spinning handshake (a thread that makes CUDA calls while the GPU spins; first-use kernel loads) apply to any integrated version.
- D55 (2026-10-31) With GPU 1 permitted for this experiment (D54), the extra expert-cache tier was priced in two steps instead of assumed: a request to GPU 1 costs 50.6 us + 14.6 us per expert (H74), and a decode model charging that latency still gives +36% to +41% for a 110-slot tier (H75). The next step is a prototype in the hybrid engine, staged so that the simplest version is built first (see H76).
- D56 (2026-10-31) The GPU 1 tier is built with policy D (at most 25 swaps per row, best uncached expert for the worst tier-1 expert, copies visible one row later), not S: D is the same shape as tier 0's pair scan, its traffic per row is bounded (S8 and S32 bursts reach 28 copies per row on average and far more at a refresh), and it passes H76 (+29.4% / +24.0% at 110 slots). S32 (+35.1% / +28.3%) stays as the variant to try afterwards. Expected cost of a mistake here: low, the tier is opt-in and off by default.

Requests to it must never use copy-engine copies (D57a, measured: -4.7% with them, +14.5% without). Its policy is H76's D with duplicates evicted first. Reversible: unset the variable.

Ideas not run, each with its reason: (a) the MTP layer's experts in GPU 1 instead of GPU 0, which would give the six main-cache slots back (guess +1..3%): the hybrid engine aborts with two CUDA backends in the scheduler (`moe_hybrid_get`), so the model's device list cannot include CUDA1 without changing that check and the draft context's cross-device copies; (b) the resident-expert matvec `mul_mat_vec_q_moe` (23% of the kernel time, 50-70% of the card's bandwidth by the H7 microbenchmark): at most +3..7% if it reached the bandwidth bound, not reachable by a tuning knob (H7), a kernel redesign; (c) a few more tier slots by lowering `LLAMA_MOE_HYBRID_G1_RESERVE_MIB` (GPU 1 holds 7,415 MiB of 8,192 with 99 slots; about 4 slots, +1%): inside the noise of one A-B-B-A and it trades away the safety margin of a card that other programs may use; (d) prefill on both GPUs (layer pipeline over segments): the KV of half the layers would sit on GPU 1 while the decode reads it from GPU 0, a redesign, not an experiment. Defaults changed with the flag `--lm-prefill-peer-gpu`: `--lm-prefill-peer-min` 8192 -> 3584 (the interleave wins below a batch of ~3,500 tokens, the split above: table in the H102 row) and `--lm-prefill-min` 256 when the peer GPU is on and the flag is not given (the tail then runs interleaved). The follower reads the leader's banks by default (`LLAMA_LM_PEER_OWN_SRC=1` reverts). Reversible: three flags / one variable.
- D71 (2026-11-01) Reading of the bit-identity bar of H102 M3.0. As written ("bit-identical against the one-GPU pass for the same request order") it FAILED on the default dynamic cache. Before dismissing the idea the implementation was checked for a bug at four levels (layer outputs, sequence state, slot bytes, bank bytes: all identical), and the cause was found outside the pass: the regrow of the expert cache lands on a different slot count when the free memory differs by 2 MiB. The bar is therefore judged at an equal cache size (frozen cache or `--hybrid-moe-slots 16`), which is a change of the measuring condition decided after seeing the dynamic result and recorded here; the bar itself (every token and every top-5 logprob) is unchanged and met 9 of 9 sizes + 6 continuations. The dynamic-cache difference stays a recorded limit: switching the second GPU on can move the text at the same level as any 2 MiB change of the free memory, and the owner should not expect the text of a greedy decode to be bit-equal with and without the follower.

They found the two causes above and are the only way to repeat that diagnosis. A process note: a pattern kill (`pkill -x -f`) was used once to stop a queue script of this session; it matched only that script, but exact PIDs are the rule.
